# Issues

**The register of everything open in this repo.** Defects, gaps, and questions
awaiting a ruling. One id per item, stable forever.

**This file is the source of truth for what is open.** Nothing is open unless it
is here. Other documents no longer carry their own open-item lists — they link
here. Long analysis may stay where it was argued (`packages/*/PROJECT_STATE.md`,
`IDEAS/*.md`); the **Detail** column points at it. What must
never happen again is an item existing in exactly one document that nobody reads.

**What belongs here:** shipped behavior that is wrong, a gap that blocks
something, a question that needs a decision. **What does not:** features and
roadmap (`IDEAS/`), settled rulings (`DECISIONS.md`), traps that are correct
behavior you must know about (`CLAUDE.md` § Live hazards), history
(`packages/*/CHANGES.md`).

**Closed items age out to [ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md).**
§ Closed below keeps the recent ones; the archive keeps the rest, verbatim.
Together they hold every id ever issued, and an id is never reused — so a
`FJS-###` named in a comment, a handoff, or a package file resolves in exactly
one of the two files. Search both before concluding an id is unknown.

## Conventions

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Id** | `FJS-###` for a defect or gap, `FJS-D##` for a decision. **Never reused, never renumbered.** Closing an item moves it to § Closed with its id. |
| **Sev** | `S1` blocker — data loss, a security hole, or cannot ship · `S2` high — silently wrong, costs a day to find · `S3` medium · `S4` low or cosmetic |
| **Status** | `open` — verified open on the date shown · `stale?` — inherited from an older audit and **not re-verified**; probe before acting · `contested` — the recorded diagnosis is doubted, see the note |
| **Verified** | The date somebody last ran it. A `stale?` row's date is when it was *written*, not confirmed. |
| **Blocked by** | A row that cannot start until another closes says `blocked by FJS-###` in its own text, and nowhere else. `fli next` takes it out of the ranking until that row closes and weighs the blocker up. Write it only where the order is real — a row that merely relates to another cites it. |
| **By hand** | A row a headless session cannot finish — a run longer than a session lasts, a check only the owner can make — says `by hand` as its own segment of its links cell. `fli next` keeps it ranked and marks it; `fix:loop` skips it. |

**A row has exactly the columns its table's header names**, and `fli
register:check` fails on anything else (`row-shape`). Both ways of getting it
wrong are silent: a row with FEWER columns is one pasted from the other table,
and the reader infers a shape from the cell count, so it is given a status
nobody wrote and no other rule can see it; a row with MORE has the excess
**dropped when the file is rendered** — in § Closed that is the *How* column, so
the row displays with no citations while they sit in the file. Links belong
inside the last declared column, joined with ` · `, and a `|` that is part of a
sentence is escaped `\|`.

`stale?` is load-bearing. The eight rows inherited from the 2026-07-31 audit
(now `IDEAS/review-coherence.md`) were re-probed on 2026-08-10 and **five of the eight were wrong**:
two were already fixed (conduit's secrets, `admin:generate`), one was never
reproducible (conduit's version skew), and two were right about a defect and
wrong about what it was — sierra's prefetch is not an auth *bypass* but a
cached 401, and caravan's row named three bugs of which two were fixed.
**Treat a `stale?` row as a lead, not a fact** (`VERIFYING.md`).

**The register also goes stale in the closing direction, which nothing here was
watching for.** `FJS-043` was fixed in the tree with a comment describing the
old defect in the past tense, and stayed `open` for eight days because closing
is a second action nobody owed. A row's `Verified` date is evidence about when
somebody last *looked*, not about whether the defect survives — 22 rows carried
`open` at 2026-08-02 when this audit ran, which is the same fiction `stale?`
exists to name, wearing the other label.

| <a id="fjs-1660"></a>FJS-1660 | litestone | **An @@export CSV writes a text cell starting with = + - @ as is, so a spreadsheet runs landed or user data as a formula** | open | 2026-10-03 | Seen by Transit Phase 3. export.js csvCell() quotes by content only: a value =HYPERLINK("https://x","Refund") or =cmd\|' /C calc'!A0 reaches the file bare, and Excel, Numbers and Sheets evaluate it when a staff member opens the extract (CWE-1236, OWASP CSV injection). An @@export is taken at the caller's standing over rows other people wrote, which is the case the guard exists for. The usual answer prefixes a TEXT cell beginning with = + - @ tab or CR with a single quote; a numeric value, and numeric-looking text such as -12.50, is left alone. That changes what a re-import reads back, so it is a ruling, not a patch: fidelity of a round trip vs. what opening the file runs. Transit's report CSV (api/src/reports/csv.ts) carries the guard as a second writer until the framework has one, ideally in toolbelt beside cells so the reader and the writer share a rule. · blocked by `FJS-D571` |

The static build passes because its pages ship no script, so the grade never sees it. Every remark or rehype plugin a config names is shipped too. Emitting the three keys by value would drop any function they hold, which is the decision. | <a id="fjs-1503"></a>FJS-1503 | junction · sierra | **A `cookie()` bearer is read over the WebSocket from the cookie the UPGRADE carried, so `FJS-D340`'s redeem — which sets the cookie after the page has loaded and the socket has opened — is invisible to every socket call, and a cookie cleared after load keeps working over the socket until the tab reloads.** | open | 2026-09-29 | **Driven in Chrome**, jazzhr Phase 3 (`.probe/phase3/ws-cookie.ts`, `drive.ts`). The applicant's page posts the link's fragment token to a raw route that `setCookie`s it (D340's shape, the first one built), and `bearerClaim({ from: cookie('jazzhr_link') })` reads it. The browser client had already opened `/ws` at boot — **for a signed-out stranger too**: the scaffold topbar reads *live* with no session, where the scaffold's own `index.mesa` comment says a socket opens only once the client holds a token. `http.ts` copies the upgrade's headers into `ws.data.headers`, which is every frame's `ctx.caller.headers`, so `cookie()` digests the upgrade's cookie for the life of the socket. **Measured, same page, straight after the redeem**: `GET /api/applications` (HTTP, sent before the socket connected) → the applicant's row; `WS find stageChanges` → `[]`, `WS find documents` → `[]`, `WS withdraw applications/2` → **404**. On screen: an application with no history, no documents, and a withdraw button that answers *not found*. After a reload (the upgrade now carries the cookie): history 1 row. **The converse is the security half**: after `POST /portal/leave` clears the cookie, with no reload, HTTP reads **0** rows and the socket still reads **1** — *forget this browser* on a shared machine leaves the open tab reading the application. Revoking the grant server-side does end it (**0**), because `bearerClaim` re-reads the grant row per call. App workaround in jazzhr: reload after redeem and after leave. What could own it: the client not preferring the socket for a caller whose credential is a cookie it cannot see; the transport re-handshaking when a raw route sets or clears a cookie a principal resolver reads; or `cookie()` documented as HTTP-only · [PLAN.md § Q3](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/PLAN.md) · [FJS-D340](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d340) · [FJS-1322](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1322) · blocked by `FJS-D548` |
| <a id="fjs-1485"></a>FJS-1485 | litestone | **Nothing declares a row personal or how long it lives, and a column staff must READ cannot be kept out of the `@@log` trail: an applicant tracker's forget rule, its PII and its report input are all comments, and every applicant's name, email and phone is copied into an append-only store at create.** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *jazzhr stressor* Phase 1, on the discovery draft ([schema.lite § Candidate](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite#L344)). A stranger's `candidate.create` lands `after: {"name":"Ada Lovelace","email":"ada@example.com","phone":"+15555550100",…}` in `db/audit/auditLogs.jsonl`. **Three halves, one missing idea** — [`IDEAS/compliance-from-the-seed.md`](IDEAS/compliance-from-the-seed.md) items 1–3, still PARTIAL, now with a product: **(1) no word for personal data.** `@pii` redirects to `@encrypted`, which the visibility table defines as *caller does not read* — a recruiter must read the name. **(2) no row for "readable, and not retained".** `@@log` redacts only `@encrypted` / `@guarded` / `@secret` (Invariant 7), and each of those also stops staff reading the column; the visibility table (`catalog.snapshot.md` § The visibility table) has no row whose answer is *the caller reads it and the trail does not keep it*. So the choice for a careful team is: audit the model and retain every applicant's PII for the trail's lifetime, or do not audit it. The trail's own `retention 90d` runs only when something calls it ([FJS-1480](#fjs-1480)). **(3) no word for a record's lifetime.** *A person whose every application was rejected more than 180 days ago is forgotten* has no spelling: no `@retain`, no `@@erase`; `@@expires` HIDES rows past a date (every read filters, `withExpired` drops it) and removes nothing; the database-level `retention` is per database, `createdAt`-only and cannot say *rejected AND older than*. **And the report is the casualty.** `StageChange` (the raw input to *median days in each stage*) is FK'd to the application and cascades with it, and `@from(…, count)` is computed at read time — so erasing a person erases the numbers the product is sold on. Whether the answer is an app-written aggregate, an anonymized tombstone, or a declaration the erase verb reads is jazzhr's Q1, built in its Phase 2 · [schema.lite § Candidate](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite#L344) · [§ StageChange](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite#L453) · [PLAN.md § Q1](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/PLAN.md) · [compliance-from-the-seed.md](IDEAS/compliance-from-the-seed.md) · [FJS-1480](#fjs-1480) **Amended 2026-09-29, measured (jazzhr Phase 2: 500 applicants, retention over 288, two subject requests, both spellings).** The report half is answered in app code: an aggregate table (spelling A) and a tombstone (spelling B) each keep *applicants by source per quarter* and *median days per stage* byte-identical across the erasure, where plain `delete()` moves every number (R1 500 → 210). **What app code could not reach is the framework's own stores**, and that is this row now: **(a) the trail gains a copy at erasure.** Every forgotten email is in the trail TWICE: in the create's `after`, and in the erasure's own line, the `delete`'s `before` (delete) or the `update`'s `before` (tombstone): 576 hits for 288 people under both spellings. **(b) the trail re-identifies what was kept, 290/290.** A tombstone keeps its id, and the trail's `candidate.create` line maps that id to name and email; spelling A's unkeyed per-stay rows match exactly one application's `stageAt` pair in the trail's `application.update` snapshots (850/850 spans), whose `candidateId` finishes the join. Coarsened to whole days and ISO weeks: still 264/290. Without the trail, 256/290 tombstoned applications are the only one of their (job, applied day). **(c) the file's freed pages** hold 209 (delete) / 90 (tombstone) of the 288 emails; `PRAGMA secure_delete = ON` through `asSystem().sql` leaves 18 and does not persist on the connection, `VACUUM` leaves 0. No word reaches either. **(d) a queued job's payload** keeps the address of a person rejected this week for caravan's `cleanupAfter` (7 d), with no verb to purge by subject. Plus FJS-1497 (a cascade is unseen by `@@log` and `FileStorage`). **Answer, in [`IDEAS/forgetting.md`](IDEAS/forgetting.md): C**, a per-column declaration the trail and an erase verb read; the retention CLOCK stays app code (a 15-line caravan job, run twice, second run 0) · [PLAN.md § Erasure map](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/PLAN.md) · [FJS-1497](#fjs-1497) **Amended 2026-09-29, measured (jazzhr Phase 3, `.probe/phase3/erase-link.sh`): the link's own trail line names the forgotten applicant, under both spellings.** An applicant with a redeemed link (`bearerClaim` over an `ApplicationGrant`, `subject: 'candidateId'`) corrects her phone; the line is `actorType: 'bearer'`, `actorId` = the grant row (FJS-D342, as ruled), `subjectId` = the candidate. After a subject request forgets her, every door answers as if the link never existed — empty 200s identical to a stranger's, 404 for her file and her withdraw, 410 for her old link identical to a garbage token's, a resend that mails nothing; the grant rows, files and freed pages are gone (tombstone: 0 pages; delete: 1) and the outbox and queue hold only `{"grantId":n}`, because the app mints the token at send time. **The trail keeps her**: the grant's line carries her email in its own `before`/`after` (the model is `@@log`'d), and its `subjectId` joins to the `candidate.create` line ("Eve Erased"). D342 was built so a revoked link is answerable as *which link did this*; after erasure the same two columns are a key to the person. forgetting.md's C covers the snapshot half (`[personal]` in the trail); `subjectId` is a second column C must say something about · blocked by `FJS-D547` |
| <a id="fjs-257"></a>FJS-257 | basecamp · outpost | **The Outpost exists and has never run on a real machine.** Was *a deploy with no Outpost reports six green steps and runs nothing*, and the scope has narrowed twice since. Basecamp's half closed 2026-08-19 — `providers/executor.ts` answers outpost / named stub / refusal, `apps.place` writes the placement three jobs read and nothing wrote, and a digest the machine reports is recorded on `Deployment.builtImage` and addressed by every later step. `packages/outpost` is the process on the other end: the route table basecamp already spoke, every route but liveness signed, build-on-target answering the digest of what it built, and a heartbeat that registers the machine as a Conduit target. basecamp's drive runs the REAL server with a fake docker runner, so the protocol both sides speak is graded rather than restated. **Three clauses of this row went false on 2026-09-08 and are struck**: *nothing installs an Outpost on a server* (cloud-init installs one on a machine basecamp bought, and `servers.issueEnrollment` prints the one command that installs one on a machine somebody else made — `docs/PROVISIONING.md` P2/P5), *there is no `pending → provisioning → installing → ready` walk* (the walk is declared on `@@transitions` and driven end to end by `verify:provision`; `ready` was deleted by [FJS-1021](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1021) and `installing` is what a first heartbeat leaves), and *the fleet shares one secret, so a compromised machine can forge another's check-in* (every machine holds a credential minted at enrollment, inbound and outbound both resolve it per machine, and `OUTPOST_SECRET` is not declared any more — P3/P5). **What remains is one claim, and it is now bigger than the row that carried it**: nothing here has run against a real machine. **The Docker-daemon clause is struck** (2026-09-26): `packages/outpost/test/docker-live.test.js` grades the inspector against a real daemon's own byte counts, read off its API socket, and the first meeting found both of the inspector's readings wrong — Docker's human sizes are decimal and were scaled by 1024, so every disk report read 2–7% fuller than the disk, and `volume prune` takes no `--format`, so the unused-volume sweep exited 125 having removed nothing. That grade covers `system df` and `volume prune`; `build`, `run` and `logs` still meet a daemon first on a fleet machine. **What stays open is the box**: the cloud-init script — the Docker install, the Bun install, the systemd unit and the enrollment exchange — has only ever been `sh -n`-parsed. It is this repo's reading of a vendor's documentation, which is the same limit `verify:stripe` names, and the only thing that closes it is a real account and a real box. Build-once-promote-a-digest is still §b for basecamp and the Outpost: V1 builds on the target, which is honest while the Outpost is co-resident with basecamp and stops being honest at the second machine. **The CLI half of digest addressing is not open and the clause saying so is struck** (2026-09-13): `04-build-api` asks the built image what it is and `06-swap` starts `imageAddress`, falling back to the tag only when the inspect fails | open | 2026-09-08 | [packages/outpost](packages/outpost/) · [executor.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/providers/executor.ts) · [enrollment.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/providers/compute/enrollment.ts) · [PROVISIONING.md](packages/basecamp/docs/PROVISIONING.md) · [IDEAS/deploy-plane.md](IDEAS/deploy-plane.md) · by hand · blocked by `FJS-1397` |

| <a id="fjs-1711"></a>FJS-1711 | mesa | **A .md file's data-fjs-loc names a line of the template the Markdown compiles to, not of the file, so alt-click on Markdown opens the wrong line** | open | 2026-10-04 | An 11-line .md stamped x.md:12:1 · compileMd renders remark output to Mesa and compiles that · the ask panel's text edit searches a .md's whole body for this reason · [compiler-md.js](packages/mesa/src/compiler-md.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1708"></a>FJS-1708 | example | **Example `verify` writes onto the seeded `SUB-3001` and its PRO plan on every run and never clears either. After a few weeks of runs the dev db breaks four checks in two drives, and re-seeding does not fix it.** Each run's `subDetail.changePlan` issues an invoice on `SUB-3001`, and `planDetail.repriced` opens a new PRO `PlanVersion`. Measured 2026-10-04: 208 invoices on `SUB-3001` and 218 versions. Three effects follow. (1) The pages ask for 50 rows newest-first, so `subDetail.changePlan` waits for an invoice count that cannot rise (this is `FJS-1376`'s cause), and `verify:account`'s *and their invoices* never sees `INV-3001`. (2) `planDetail.newWindowEmpty` never sees version 1, the one `SUB-3001` holds. (3) The unpaid invoices lapse `SUB-3001` to `pastDue` through dunning, which reddens `subDetail.stopRenewing`. `db:seed` upserts and deletes nothing, so it heals none of this. `verify:account` also throws before its cleanup runs, which leaves `SUB-ACC*`/`SUB-OTH*`/`INV-A*`/`INV-Q*` behind on every run. Done when both drives mint what they change, or put it back, and both hold on a db that has been driven for a month. | open | 2026-10-04 | Cleared by hand on 2026-10-04: the invoices and the run rows deleted, `SUB-3001` recovered with `recoverIfClear`. Example `verify` then passed `subDetail.changePlan` and `subDetail.stopRenewing`. `planDetail.newWindowEmpty` stays red because the versions were left alone. Unrelated to the billing lift (`FJS-D575`), which passes the same steps. |

| <a id="fjs-1679"></a>FJS-1679 | mesa | **A $: statement runs where it is written, so a const it calls that is declared below it is in its TDZ; Svelte runs every $: after the whole script** | open | 2026-10-04 | Seen 2026-10-03 in Transit Phase 6, porting my.maid.tech report 70 (a Svelte 4 template). Repro: <script> export let n = 2; $: doubled = twice(n); const twice = (x) => x * 2 </script><p>{doubled}</p> — renderComponent throws 'Cannot access twice before initialization'. Svelte 4 hoists every reactive declaration to run after all top-level statements, in dependency order, so the original runs there. Mesa creates the derived signal at its source position and computes it eagerly (runtime createWritableSignal -> _recompute). Workaround in transit/fixtures/maid/reports/demand-seasonality/template.mesa: the const arrow became a function declaration. Expected: either Svelte's order, or a compile error naming the $: and the later const, not a runtime TDZ. |
| <a id="fjs-1677"></a>FJS-1677 | mesa | **A top-level name that is an Object.prototype member (valueOf, toString, constructor…) crashes compile: 'Spread syntax requires ...iterable[Symbol.iterator]'** | open | 2026-10-04 | Seen by the transit stressor (Phase 6, 2026-10-03): a route declaring 'const valueOf = (name, p) => …' failed in Vite with Mesa's internal TypeError and no line. Minimal: '<script> let n = 1; const toString = () => String(n) </script><p>{toString()}</p>' fails the same way. Cause: packages/mesa/src/compiler.js topoSort (~8766) reads 'extraDeps?.[v.name]' off a plain object, so for v.name 'valueOf' it gets Object.prototype.valueOf and spreads a function. nameMap at the line above is the same shape. Use Object.hasOwn / a Map / Object.create(null) for both. Workaround in transit: renamed the binding. |
| <a id="fjs-1676"></a>FJS-1676 | mesa | **$: ({ a, b } = obj) declares nothing: compiles clean, then 'a is not defined' at render, with a hint that blames the browser** | open | 2026-10-04 | Seen by the transit stressor (Phase 6, 2026-10-03) porting my.maid.tech's Svelte 4 report 68. Svelte 4 implicitly declares every name a reactive destructuring assignment writes; '$: ({ accounts, attention, summary, complete, inProgress } = r)' is idiomatic there. Mesa compiles it with no error or warning, emits no declaration for the names, and the server render throws 'accounts is not defined' with the RULE 19 hint ('does not exist during a server render — a build has no browser… move it into $.onMount'), which sends the author the wrong way. The AGENTS.md table documents '$: sel = options[0]' (a single name) only. Either declare the pattern's names as writable derived (one memo, a binding per name), or refuse it at compile naming '$: accounts = r.accounts'. Not FJS-021 (that was invalid JS for already-declared lets). Workaround in transit's fixtures/maid/reports/form-responses-by-month/template.mesa: one $: per name. |
| <a id="fjs-1675"></a>FJS-1675 | mesa | **{@const [a, b] = expr} compiles to invalid JS: const $$_const_[a, b] = …** | open | 2026-10-04 | Seen by the transit stressor (Phase 6, 2026-10-03) porting my.maid.tech's Svelte 4 report 68: inside {#each}, '{@const [bg, fg] = heat(cellIntensity(a, m))}' compiles without an error or warning, and the module then fails to parse at import: 'const $$_const_[bg, fg] = $$runtime.createMemo(() => heat(...))' — Expected ';' but found '['. compile() accepts it, so a save-time compile check passes and the first render throws. Svelte allows an array or object pattern in {@const}. Either support a destructuring pattern (one memo, then a binding per name) or refuse it at compile naming '{@const pair = …}' and pair[0]. Workaround in transit: fixtures/maid/reports/form-responses-by-month/template.mesa binds the pair and indexes it. |
| <a id="fjs-1674"></a>FJS-1674 | litestone | **The client schema drops a read-policied column from required, so a create form calls a NOT NULL column optional** | open | 2026-10-04 | Found by Transit Phase 5 (fjs-prototypes/transit, 2026-10-03). packages/litestone/src/jsonschema.js:607: under audience 'client', a field with @allow('read', expr) is rewritten to anyOf [type, null] and left out of 'required', because its value may be absent on a READ for a caller the policy refuses. The browser builds its WRITE form from that same schema, so 'may be absent when read' becomes 'may be omitted when written'. Measured with generateJsonSchema over transit's db/schema.lite: client Template.required = [name, report, kind] and Source.required = [name, kind]; system Template.required = [name, report, kind, source] and Source.required = [name, kind, schemaText]. Both columns are non-optional Strings with @allow('read', auth().isStaff). On screen: <Form> labels Source and Schema text '(Optional)', and a blank one passes the client validator and is refused only by the server's NOT NULL. Transit has two sightings (Source.schemaText, Phase 2; Template.source, Phase 5). The read half and the write half need separate answers: optional for a read is right, required for a create is still true. |
| <a id="fjs-1673"></a>FJS-1673 | cli | **findApps keys on db/schema.lite alone, so a workspace walk (fli check from a root above, release-view, runnables) never finds a web-only app** | open | 2026-10-04 | Found fixing FJS-1617; checks.js's rules now accept a manifest plus a surface, but runnables.js findApps (line 80) still demands the schema · [cli core/runnables.js](packages/cli/core/runnables.js) |

| <a id="fjs-1669"></a>FJS-1669 | toolbelt | **make() seeds a foreign key the server fills as null, so a create form with no record sends ownerId: null and @default(auth().id) never applies** | open | 2026-10-04 | Seen by Transit Phase 4 (Subscription.ownerId String @default(auth().id) @immutable, a relation to User). createMakeFromSchema (packages/toolbelt/src/jsonschema/jsonschema.js ~L184) seeds every foreign key as null in the fkFields branch, which runs BEFORE the skip for a NOT NULL column the caller is not asked for (demanded && !nullable && !demanded.has(key), ~L197) — the skip FJS-1162 added for exactly this, the server's stamp. A key that is both is seeded null, the null is sent, and the server answers 400 'ownerId must be a string' as a form-level error on a form whose every visible control is valid. except={['ownerId']} hides the control and still sends the key. Fix is order: test the server-filled skip before the FK branch. Transit works around it in web/src/resources/Subscription.mesa by seeding make() minus ownerId. | <a id="fjs-1665"></a>FJS-1665 | email-kit | **A row component (Heading, Text, KeyValue, Image, DataTable) inside a Column renders, and everything after it runs the client's full width** | open | 2026-10-03 | Seen by Transit Phase 3 (RevenueReport.email.mesa). Five of the kit's components render a <tr>, so they belong in a Section or a Card; Column renders a <td>. Wrapped as <Row><Column><DataTable/></Column></Row> — the README's own shape for content — the row lands inside a cell, the parser moves it out, and the DataTable lays out 668px wide in a 700px window instead of 568px inside the 600px container. Nothing warns, the README does not say which components are rows, and a string check over the output cannot see it because the server render has already moved the rows. slot-guard.js exists for the sibling mistake (children to a childless component); a row component could say the same when its parent is a Column. |
| <a id="fjs-1657"></a>FJS-1657 | litestone · sierra · ui | **No schema word makes a String multi-line, so a column holding code or an address renders as a one-line input that cannot hold a newline; only @markdown reaches a textarea** | open | 2026-10-04 | Found by the transit stressor's drive (PLAN Phase 2), 2026-10-03. Source.schemaText holds a landing schema as .lite text, many lines, and the generated settings form (ui Form over sierra formFields) renders it as <input type=text>: sierra field-rules.js answers control 'textarea' for a string only when contentMediaType is text/markdown (@markdown), and every other String is 'input'. A one-line input's value has its newlines stripped by the browser's value sanitization, so an admin who edits the schema there gets one line, and in .lite the first // comment then swallows the rest. Measured: saving the form WITHOUT touching the field leaves the row byte for byte (the form sends what changed; the drive asserts it), so nothing is corrupted silently — the field is just unusable for its one purpose. @markdown is the wrong word (the text is not Markdown and would render as it), and an app cannot target one column through registerControl because a resolver is handed the rule alone, with no model or field name. Wanted: a schema word for plain multi-line text (or code, which would reach ui's CodeInput), derived into control 'textarea'/'code' the way @markdown is. Transit leaves schemaText as it is until then; it is written by the seed and by admins through the API. · Second sighting, Transit Phase 5 (2026-10-03): Template.source holds a Mesa template, so the generated one-line box cannot edit it. Template's default form (web/src/resources/Template.mesa) is hand-written for that one column, with CodeInput, and names every field to do it. · **Code half answered 2026-10-03:** `@syntax(lang)` replaces @markdown — `x-syntax` in the JSON Schema, control `code` (CodeInput in that language) for every language but `md`, which stays a textarea. Both sightings are code, and Transit now declares them (`schemaText String @syntax(lite)`, `source String @syntax(mesa)`, and Report's `query`/`transform`/`template`): its hand-written Template form and its `registerControl` claim on `Source.schemaText` are gone, and the generated `code` control is numbered (2026-10-04, Transit `verify` green through both forms). **Still open:** plain multi-line text (an address), which is not a syntax and has no sighting yet. |
| <a id="fjs-1656"></a>FJS-1656 | litestone | **A view's @@sql reads a @guarded column in plaintext, and an @encrypted one as ciphertext, to any caller who passes the view's gate; nothing at build says so** | open | 2026-10-03 | Found by the transit stressor (PLAN Phase 2, L9) proving sensitive fields in a landing schema held in a row. Probed 2026-10-03 with createClient({ untrusted: true, encryptionKey }) and the app's gate plugin: model Person { id String @id; score Int @guarded; email String @encrypted; @@gate("4.8.8.8"); @@allow('read', auth().isStaff) } and view personScore { id String; score Int; email String; @@sql("SELECT id, score, email FROM person"); @@gate("4"); @@allow('read', auth().isStaff) }. A level-4 staff reader gets { id } from person.findFirst() and { id, score: 7, email: 'v2.…' } from personScore.findFirst(). @guarded is documented as 'absent from reads … outside asSystem()', and FJS-D52 keeps raw SQL behind asSystem(), but a view's @@sql is raw SQL that runs for every reader the view's own gate admits, and its columns take no attributes (FJS-1643), so the protection cannot be restated on it either. Not an escalation inside one schema (whoever writes the view writes the @guarded too, and could drop it), so S3: a silent hole in what the word promises, in a trusted schema as much as a row-held one. Every client already prepares each view at build (FJS-1632); that is where a view whose result columns come from a @guarded, @secret, @hashed or @encrypted column could be refused by name, or the column marked protected on the view. Transit's landing schemas keep sensitive columns out of every view until then. |
| <a id="fjs-1655"></a>FJS-1655 | litestone | **@secret in a schema with no logger database drops its @log half without a word, and an untrusted schema can never declare one** | open | 2026-10-03 | Found by the transit stressor (PLAN Phase 2, L9), probing @encrypted/@guarded/@secret in a landing schema held in a Source row and built with createClient({ untrusted: true }). docs/encryption.md § @secret says it 'expands at parse time to @encrypted + @guarded + @log(audit). Every read and write is logged to the audit logger database.' The parser's own comment (core/parser.js, case 'secret') says '(log only if logger db declared)', and that is what happens: with no logger database the parsed field's attributes are ['secret','encrypted','guarded'] and no read or write of it is ever logged, with no warning at parse, at build or at the first read. The encryption and the guard both hold (probed: ciphertext on disk, absent from a level-4 read, refused in a where, refused on a write outside asSystem()). An untrusted schema refuses every database block by name (FJS-1633), so in a schema held in a row @secret can only ever be @encrypted @guarded spelled shorter, while its docs promise an audit trail. Either refuse @secret where no logger database is declared (naming @encrypted @guarded as the honest spelling), or let the host hand an untrusted client an audit sink it owns; and correct the docs to say which. Transit's landing schemas use @encrypted @guarded spelled out until then. |
| <a id="fjs-1648"></a>FJS-1648 | ui | **A prop a kit component does not declare lands on its root element as an HTML attribute, so <EmptyState sub="…"> renders no text and says nothing** | open | 2026-10-03 | Found 2026-10-03 (Transit). RevenueReport wrote <EmptyState title=… sub="Pick another currency…"> by analogy with SectionHeader and StatCard, which both take sub; EmptyState's body is its default slot, so sub rode {...$attributes} onto the div as sub="…" and the sentence never rendered, through a browser drive that asserted the title. Nothing warned: not the compiler, not fli check, not the browser. A lowercase name that is not a known HTML/ARIA attribute or data-*, passed to a kit component that declares no such prop, is almost always a misremembered prop. Wants a compile-time or dev warning naming the component's props, or a fli check rule over kit components' declared exports. |
| <a id="fjs-1645"></a>FJS-1645 | litestone | **loadRows holds every record of its source in memory before writing, so a 200,000-row CSV of 12.6 MB peaks at 1.4 GB RSS: about 110 times the file** | open | 2026-10-03 | Measured in the transit stressor, 2026-10-03 (PLAN M1), the day loadRows landed: orders.csv (5,000 rows) 206 ms / 125 MB peak RSS into :memory:, 237 ms / 128 MB into a file; orders-wide.csv (200,000 rows) 4.85 s / 1.39 GB into :memory:, 4.97 s / 1.41 GB into a file. The landing target costs nothing (file vs memory within 3%); the memory is loadRows reading the whole CSV into records, then a second array of coerced rows, before the first batch is written. The load needs only the keys it has seen to decide a repeat, so it can take an async iterable of records (or read CSV text incrementally) and write as it goes. Phase 2's REST source arrives page by page and wants the same shape. |
| <a id="fjs-1643"></a>FJS-1643 | litestone | **A view's columns take no field attributes, so a view summing a @money column cannot say its result is money: the parser refuses 'revenue Int @money(field: currency)' inside a view with 'Expected IDENT, got @'** | open | 2026-10-03 | Seen in the transit stressor, 2026-10-03: a landing schema's view customerRevenue sums Order.amount (Int @money(field: currency)) per customer and currency. Declaring the view's revenue column '@money(field: currency)' fails the parse at the '@', so the column is a bare Int of minor units. Everything @money buys a model column is then lost on the view: the client's money formatting, the generated form/table rendering, and fli check's money-rendered-raw, which cannot see that a report printing revenue raw prints cents. A view is the read path T1 reports are built on, so every report over a money aggregate restates the currency by hand. The view's other access attributes (@@gate, @@allow) are accepted; only field-level ones are not. |

| <a id="fjs-1640"></a>FJS-1640 | cli | **fli check passes an app whose migration history does not build its schema; migration-history, the rule the app's AGENTS.md cites for it, fires only on a Dockerfile app with no migration at all** | open | 2026-10-03 | Seen in the transit stressor, 2026-10-03. | <a id="fjs-1637"></a>FJS-1637 | cli · junction | **From the framework tree, fli new defaults to --source npm and writes an app the published packages cannot typecheck: the scaffold's auth-cleanup plugin has a work() hook that published junction 0.1.8 does not declare, so bun run check is red on day one** | open | 2026-10-03 | Measured 2026-10-03, *transit stressor* Phase 0: an app scaffolded without `--source local` installs `@frontierjs/*@latest` (junction 0.1.8 from npm) and `bun run check` fails at typecheck: *api/src/app.ts(57,9): TS2353 'work' does not exist in type 'PluginInput'*. | <a id="fjs-1636"></a>FJS-1636 | cli | **fli new <name> --here ignores <name> in silence and names the app after the folder: package name, cli namespace and README all take the directory's name** | open | 2026-10-03 | Measured 2026-10-03, *transit stressor* Phase 0: `mkdir transit-next && cd transit-next && fli new transit --here --source local --full --yes` → `package.json` `"name": "@frontierjs/transit-next"`, `.fli.json` `defaultNamespace: "transit-next"`, `cli/src/routes/transit-next/`, and the closing line *✓ transit-next created*. | <a id="fjs-1625"></a>FJS-1625 | ui | **Table.mesa draws each header cell from col.label alone, with no snippet for a header, so a header cannot hold a control — a select-all checkbox, a remove or freeze button on a column, a resize handle — and a caller that needs one moves it out of the header or stops using Table** | open | 2026-10-02 | Found 2026-10-02, *dragonfly, the JSON-grid stressor*, Phase 2. [Table.mesa](packages/ui/components/display/Table.mesa)'s <thead> is `{#each columns as col (col.name)}` → a <th> holding `col.label`, a sort button when `col.sortable`, or a visually-hidden label when `col.hideLabel`; the body has a `row` snippet and the header has nothing to match it. The grid dragonfly ports (SRC `lib/Grid.svelte`) puts five things in its header row: a select-all checkbox with an indeterminate state over the row checkboxes, and per column a sort toggle, a freeze toggle, a remove-column button and a resize handle. In the port, select-all and remove-column move to a bar above the table: the checkbox is no longer in the column it governs, and removing a column is a pick from a list instead of a button on the column. Phase 4's freeze and resize need the header too. Asked for: a `header` snippet, `(col) => …`, rendered inside the <th> the component already writes, so aria-sort and the width stay the component's · [Table.mesa](packages/ui/components/display/Table.mesa) |
| <a id="fjs-1624"></a>FJS-1624 | ui | **An editable <Json> always keeps its own undo stack — ⌘Z bound on its root, ↶/↷ buttons in its corner — and takes no way to hand history to the page, so a tree that is one editor among several over the same document is a second stack beside the page's** | open | 2026-10-02 | Found 2026-10-02, *dragonfly, the JSON-grid stressor*, Phase 2 (PLAN.md Q2). [Json.mesa](packages/ui/components/display/Json.mesa) under `editable` keeps `past`/`future` (HISTORY = 50), renders the two history buttons whenever `canEdit`, and `onGridKey` calls `onHistoryKey` for every keydown on the tree, with `preventDefault` and no opt-out. Its own comment says the binding is to the TREE so a page with its own history keeps the shortcut, but a page that owns history needs the shortcut INSIDE the tree too: dragonfly opens a <Json editable> in a grid cell beside a JSON text box, and both write one document. ⌘Z in the tree undoes the tree's last edit and announces it through `onchange` as a new write, which the page's stack records as one more step; the page's ⌘Z then lands in the tree as `somebody else's edit`, which the tree's `remember(draft)` pushes onto its own `past` — so each stack can undo the other's undo, and the ↶ in the cell walks a history that does not match the page's. Asked for: `history={false}` (no stack, no buttons, no key binding; every write still announced), so the caller's stack is the only one · [Json.mesa](packages/ui/components/display/Json.mesa) · **Measured** 2026-10-02 in dragonfly, Chrome 150 over CDP, a <Json editable> open in a grid cell beside the page's own history (which takes ⌘Z in the capture phase, so the tree's binding never sees it). The filed mechanism was a guess, and half of it is wrong; what happens is this. (1) Edit city to `Austin TX` in the tree, press the TREE's ↶: the city goes back, and the page records that as a new write. The page's Undo then puts `Austin TX` back — it undoes the tree's undo — and a second page Undo is needed to take the edit away: two presses for one edit, the first going the wrong way. (2) Edit, then page ⌘Z: the city goes back, and the tree's ↶ stays enabled; pressing it announces the document the page already holds, changes nothing, and disables itself — a dead press. The tree's `$: value` handler drops its draft when the announced document comes back, so a value arriving from outside is not pushed onto `past` here; the page's restore is invisible to the tree's stack, which is why (2) is a dead press and not a redo. Both are the same defect: two stacks over one document, and the caller cannot turn the tree's off. |
| <a id="fjs-1623"></a>FJS-1623 | toolbelt | **toolbelt/json cannot write a change back into the text a document was parsed from: tryParse answers a value with no source range, and every writer (setIn, removeIn, insertIn, renameKey) answers a value, so the only way back to text is format() of the whole document — one cell edit rewrites every line, a condensed document comes back pretty-printed, and 78.0 comes back as 78** | open | 2026-10-02 | Found 2026-10-02, *dragonfly, the JSON-grid stressor*, Phase 2 (PLAN.md Q1, write half). Where the text is the record — a JSON box beside a grid, a config file, a fixture someone hand-formatted — a write from the grid must change only the bytes of the value it changed. [tryParse](packages/toolbelt/src/json/json.js) is `JSON.parse` in a try and answers `{ ok, value }` or an error position, never where a value sits in the text; nothing else in the module reads text. The Svelte app dragonfly ports (SRC `stores/data.js:134` `syncToText`) re-serializes the whole document at two-space indent on every cell edit and reorders each row's keys to the column order, which is what any caller of this module would end up writing. Measured in SRC (PLAN.md § Port ledger M2): on the 10,000-row `wide` fixture condensed first, 1 line becomes 220,002; on `ragged`, 11,113 of 11,123 lines differ for a one-cell edit. What is missing is a write that takes the text, the document it parses to and the next document (which setIn and friends already make share every untouched branch, so the walk can skip by identity) and answers the text with only the changed spans replaced, new members laid out in the style of their siblings. dragonfly writes it locally as `patchText` in web/src/doc.js · [toolbelt json.js](packages/toolbelt/src/json/json.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1622"></a>FJS-1622 | ui | **Table.mesa keys its rows by position, {#each rows as r, i (i)}, and takes no key, so state a row's snippet holds stays at the index when a row is inserted or removed — an open nested cell or a focused input moves to the next record — and a delete at row 0 rewrites every row after it** | open | 2026-10-02 | Found 2026-10-02, *dragonfly, the JSON-grid stressor*, Phase 2 (Q4). [Table.mesa:188](packages/ui/components/display/Table.mesa) is `{#each rows as r, i (i)}` and no prop reaches the key expression, while mesa's own guide says an unkeyed each keeps focus, an uncontrolled input or an animation with the POSITION and to write `(item.id)` ([mesa AGENTS.md](packages/mesa/AGENTS.md) § gotchas). A caller of Table cannot follow that rule. In dragonfly a row's snippet holds an open <Json> tree, whose expanded nodes are its own state: removing row 0 hands block 0 row 1's item, every later block gets its neighbour's, and the open tree stays at index 2 showing the next record. The fix is a key the caller passes, a field name or `(r, i) => …`, falling back to the index — the shape `{#each}` already takes. Graded by dragonfly's M4 (PLAN.md § Measurements) · [Table.mesa](packages/ui/components/display/Table.mesa) |
| <a id="fjs-1621"></a>FJS-1621 | ui · toolbelt | **CodeInput re-highlights its whole buffer and rebuilds its whole paint on every keystroke, so a keystroke costs the size of the document rather than the size of the edit: 37.7 s to paint for one character in a 4.2 MB JSON text, against 1.6 s for the same page with a bare <textarea> and 10.8 s for the Svelte app it replaces** | open | 2026-10-02 | **Measured** 2026-10-01, *dragonfly, the JSON-grid stressor*, M1 (one real keystroke through CDP's input pipeline into the first string value, input to next paint, median of 5, Vite dev, Chrome 150) on the wide fixture (10,000 rows × 20 keys, 220,002 lines). With `<CodeInput language="json">`: **37,743 ms** median, runs 35.5 → 37.1 → 37.7 → 51.7 → 64.0 s, climbing; the CPU profile is GC 13.2 s, set innerHTML 1.6 s, glow parseRow 1.1 s, DOM remove 0.6 s, encode 0.3 s. The same page with `<textarea>` in its place: **1,557 ms**, of which JS is about 70 ms. Loading wide by paste: 34.2 s with CodeInput, 11.1 s without. At 1,000 rows (22,000 lines) Chrome's own counters for one keystroke: script 447 ms, style 420 ms, layout 1,633 ms, and **+250,011 DOM nodes** — the paint, rebuilt; with a bare textarea, script 11 ms. The cause is CodeInput.mesa:136–141: `painted = glow(paintable, …)` is derived from the whole `value` and handed to `{@html}` in one <pre>, so every input re-tokenizes every line and replaces every node under it. glow is already line-oriented — parseSyntax walks lines and carries state only for a block comment, which JSON has none of — so the paint can be one element per line, re-glowed and replaced only for the lines between the first and last that changed; a viewport window is the further step for a document past what layout can hold. JsonInput is CodeInput underneath and inherits the same cost, so any `Json` column over a few hundred kB is the same box. The miss is dragonfly's Phase 1 grade, PLAN.md § Port ledger M1 · [ui forms/CodeInput.mesa](packages/ui/components/forms/CodeInput.mesa) · [toolbelt glow/glow.js](packages/toolbelt/src/glow/glow.js) · **The climb across runs is not a leak** (2026-10-02, dragonfly). The five M1 runs are five keys in one tab. At 1,000 rows with HeapProfiler.collectGarbage after each key, the DOM returns to exactly 436,542 nodes and the JS heap to 88–89 MB after every one of 8 keys, at a flat 1.9–2.2 s a key. Without the forced GC, each key leaves the old paint behind, +206,008 nodes, and the count builds to 1.67 M (about 4× the live DOM) before Chrome collects it. So the 35 → 64 s climb on wide is the paint's garbage building up faster than the collector clears it: the same cost per key, compounding. A fix that stops rebuilding the paint removes both. |
| <a id="fjs-1619"></a>FJS-1619 | toolbelt | **toolbelt/json has no way to keep the identity of what a re-parse did not change, so every keystroke in a JSON text hands a Mesa view a document in which every object is new, and an {#each} over its rows redraws all of them for a one-character edit** | open | 2026-10-02 | **Measured** 2026-10-01, *dragonfly, the JSON-grid stressor* (Phase 1: a CodeInput over a read-only grid, the text parsed on every input). Mesa's {#each} hands a row its item through a signal that compares with === (runtime.js $$eachBlock _makeBlock → rebind → setItem), so a row handed an equal object that is not the same object re-runs every cell in it. JSON.parse answers only new objects. With the first 1,000 rows of the wide fixture (20 primitive keys), one keystroke in a string value ran every cell of every row; the 1,000-row CPU profile showed the grid's share as Mesa flush work spread over _runNode/_recompute, and the handler took 642 ms with CodeInput and 55 ms without it. A 30-line walk that hands back prev's node wherever next equals it (positional for arrays; same keys in the same order for objects, since key order is the only order a JSON object has; defineProperty so a parsed own __proto__ stays a key) costs 16 ms at 1,000 rows and 52 ms at 10,000, and after it the flush for one keystroke on 10,000 rows is 46 derived nodes and 9 blocks: only the edited row redraws. The pieces are already in the kit and do not reach this: sameValue (json.js:601) answers equal-or-not and keeps nothing; Json.mesa compares documents by value for undo and keeps nothing either. Any view over text the person edits needs it: JsonInput's onvalue hands its caller a fresh parse per keystroke too. Written locally as keepUnchanged in fjs-prototypes/dragonfly/web/src/doc.js; the fix is that function in toolbelt/json beside sameValue · [toolbelt json/json.js](packages/toolbelt/src/json/json.js) · [mesa runtime.js](packages/mesa/src/runtime.js) |

| <a id="fjs-1566"></a>FJS-1566 | portal | **Portal, the search stressor: a page that leaves <head> unclosed is captured as ok with 0 words, because the reader counts head as a skipped element and HTMLRewriter never ends it before <body>, so the whole body is skipped** | open | 2026-09-29 | Found seeding the knight persona (2026-09-29). https://lexi-lambda.github.io/blog/2019/11/05/parse-don-t-validate/ opens <head>, then <meta>/<link>/<script>, then <body> with no </head> (valid HTML: the end tag is optional, and <body> implies it). parsePage on the fetched 44 KB answered 0 words; the same page from <body> on, or the <article> alone, answered 4,080. Both readers share the shape: SKIP lists head and counts depth by skip++ / e.onEndTag(skip--), so an implied end never lowers it. Text: [parse.ts:131](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/pages/parse.ts) (SKIP at :64); copy: [article.ts:107](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/pages/article.ts) (SKIP at :28). The Page is written status ok, text and html null, words 0, so Read later opens an empty copy, the library search never finds the page, and nothing on the page says the capture failed. A fixed parser will not reach these rows by itself: capture.ts:71 counts words != null as having a copy, so a link check never recaptures a 0-word page; staleParses (reparse from snapshot) would, if PARSER is bumped. Fix: treat <body> (or the first body-only element) as the end of head, or drop head from SKIP and skip only its children (title, meta, link, script, style are already skipped or empty) |

| <a id="fjs-1517"></a>FJS-1517 | litestone · sierra | **A `@capability` column is charged to its grant whenever the payload NAMES it, `null` and unchanged included, and a generated create form names every column `make()` seeded — so on a model with one, the generated create form is refused for everybody who does not hold that column's grant, for a field the form does not even show.** | open | 2026-09-29 | **Driven in Chrome** (jazzhr Phase 4, `.probe/phase4/drive.ts` step `draft-offer`): the recruiter's generated offer form, `<Form resource={offers} only={['title','salary','startOn','terms']}>` over `offers.make({ applicationId, jobId })`, answers *"Offer.signedName" requires the capability "Offer.signedName"* on submit, and no offer is written. `Offer.signedName` is `@capability` so that only the candidate's link may sign (the grid's column tier, the one spelling for *may sign and nothing else*). **Measured on a bare `$setAuth`** (`.probe/phase4/capability-null.ts`), a caller holding `Offer.create` + `Offer.update`: create without the key → ok; create with `signedName: null` → refused; update `{ title, signedName: null }` on a row whose `signedName` is already null → refused. Two halves: litestone's `_checkUpdate` (and the create path) grades a `@capability` key by presence, where *writing* the column is what the grant is about and a null create or an unchanged value writes nothing; and sierra's create posts the whole `make()` record, `only` narrowing what is SHOWN and not what is SENT (its patch sends only changed keys, which is why an edit form on the same model works). | <a id="fjs-1516"></a>FJS-1516 | litestone | **On a `@@capabilities` model, a `@system` column the APPLICATION names on a write (`system: ['respondedAt']`) is charged to the CALLER's `Model.update` grant, so a principal who holds only a move cannot have the app stamp anything beside it: the candidate's accept, which the app timestamps, is a 403 asking the candidate for `Offer.update`.** | open | 2026-09-29 | **Measured over HTTP** (jazzhr Phase 4, `.probe/phase4/offer.sh` step 27: the link holder's `accept` → 403 *"Offer.update" requires the capability "Offer.update"*) **and on a bare `$setAuth`** (`.probe/phase4/system-grid.ts`): a bearer holding `Offer.accept`, `Offer.decline` and `Offer.signedName` may `update({ data: { status: 'accepted', signedName } })` and may `update({ data: { status: 'declined' } })`, and the same two writes plus `respondedAt` with `system: ['respondedAt']` are each refused for `Offer.update`. On a model with no grid the same shape passes (`Candidate`: `phone` plus `forgottenAt` with `system: ['forgottenAt']`). Cause, read: `CapabilityPlugin._checkUpdate` (`plugins/capability.js:87`) sorts payload keys into `@capability` columns, move fields and *ungraded*, and charges any ungraded key to `Model.update`; it never reads `args.system`, so a column the app supplies counts as one the caller edits. `@system` means *the application writes it, not the caller* (litestone AGENTS.md § access words), and naming it on the call is how the app says so, which the gate and the column rules honour and the grid does not. The ways around it are each worse: hand the stranger `Offer.update`, or write the stamp as a second `asSystem()` write, which is a second trail line (measured over HTTP: `actorType: 'system'`, still `actorId` = the grant and `subjectId` = the candidate) and splits what should be one line binding the move, the sealed terms, the signature and the time. jazzhr takes the second · [capability.js `_checkUpdate`](packages/litestone/src/plugins/capability.js) · [FJS-1245](#fjs-1245) · [jazzhr offers.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/api/src/services/offers.service.ts) |

| <a id="fjs-1513"></a>FJS-1513 | litestone · cli | **`migrate dev` writes a migration it marks BLOCKED (a NOT NULL column with no default, its rebuild commented out), then APPLIES it and records it applied, so the dev database is half-migrated and the file can no longer be fixed in place; the next run blames `db push`, which nobody ran, and advises `litestone db reset`, which is not a command.** | open | 2026-09-29 | **Measured**, jazzhr Phase 4 (`fli db:migrate`, which runs `litestone migrate dev`). Adding `jobId Int @immutable` + relation to `Offer` (one table rebuild beside another for `Interview`): the file `20260929043234_migration.sql` carries `-- "offer": rebuild BLOCKED — adds NOT NULL column(s) with no DEFAULT` with the rebuild commented out — a good refusal, with the three ways out — and in the same command it is applied (`✓ 20260929043234_migration.sql (11ms)`: the Interview rebuild ran, the Offer one did not), recorded in `_litestone_migrations`, and followed by *the migration history does not build the schema this app declares*. Hand-writing the Offer copy into the file, as the file itself advises, then changes nothing: the file is applied, `migrate status` reports *modified (checksum mismatch — edited after apply)*, and `migrate dev` now says *`db push` writes tables and no file, so a pushed change lives here* — the same wrong blame as [FJS-1490](#fjs-1490), from a different cause — and offers *Start clean: `litestone db reset`*, which answers *Unknown db subcommand "reset". Available: push* (the string exists only in that advice, `tools/cli.js:901`). jazzhr's dev database was empty, so it was deleted and the history replayed (6 applied, clean). On a database with rows, the way out is by hand. Expected: a file the generator marks blocked is written and NOT applied, and the command exits naming the file to edit · [cli.js:901](packages/litestone/src/tools/cli.js) · [FJS-1490](#fjs-1490) · [jazzhr migration](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/migrations/main/20260929043234_migration.sql) |
| <a id="fjs-1512"></a>FJS-1512 | litestone | **A sealing model has one freeze instant, the seal, so a column that must be written AT a later move and frozen from then — a signature, written by the accept after the terms were sealed at approve — has no word: `@immutable` freezes it before it can be written, a second `@seals` is refused, and the nearest spelling leaves the signature rewritable after acceptance.** | open | 2026-09-29 | **Measured**, jazzhr Phase 4 on a bare `$setAuth` (`.probe/phase4/sign.ts`, `sign-once.ts`). `Offer` is `approve: draft -> approved @seals` over `@immutable` terms (the seal must be at approve: sealing at accept leaves the terms editable while `sent`, Phase 0), and `signedName String? @capability`, which only the candidate's link holds. (1) **As declared**, signing and accepting are two writes, a signed offer can be declined (`declined`, `signedName: 'Ada Four'`), an accept needs no signature, and after `accepted` the link holder rewrites the signature (`'Ada One'` → `'Somebody Else'`, 200). (2) **`signedName @immutable`**: refused at `sent` with `SealedDocumentError` — the column froze at approve, before anybody could sign — and the one-write sign+accept is refused as *"Offer.accept" was refused by an @@allow/@@deny policy on update*, which names a POLICY for what is the seal's refusal. `fli db:explain @immutable` says *written once, at create, and never again by anybody*, which is not what it does on a sealing model. (3) **`accept: sent -> accepted @seals` beside approve's**: refused at parse (*already sealed by 'approve' … seals nothing*), correctly for what `@seals` means. (4) **What jazzhr ships**: one write carrying `status: 'accepted'` + `signedName` (graded as the `accept` move plus the `Offer.signedName` grant; the trail line has `transition: 'accept'`, the sealed terms and the name in one `after`), `@@check("(status = 'accepted') = (signedName IS NOT NULL)")` (refuses sign-alone, unsigned accept, sign+decline), and `@allow('write', status == 'sent')` on the field, which turns a later rewrite by the link holder into a **silent 200 no-op** — and `asSystem()`, a job or `fli tinker` still rewrites it (`'Mallory'`, measured). A candidate spelling: a move-scoped freeze, e.g. `signedName String? @immutable(after: accept)` or `@sealedBy(accept)`, frozen from the named move's target the way `@immutable` is from the seal · [jazzhr schema.lite `Offer`](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite) · [seal.js](packages/litestone/src/core/seal.js) · [FJS-1482](#fjs-1482) · [PLAN.md § Q4](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/PLAN.md) · blocked by `FJS-D568` |

The kit's own comment says a raw ref reaching a form is *not the caller's fault* and is reported — here nothing was read at all. | <a id="fjs-1486"></a>FJS-1486 | litestone | **`@@capabilities` covers `create`, and a stranger holds no list — so a drop box cannot opt into the grid. On a model anybody may create, the grid refuses the stranger by name, and the grid is the only spelling for *the stranger may make this one move and no other*.** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *jazzhr stressor* Phase 1, on the discovery draft: `Application` is `@@gate("0")` + `@@capabilities`, with `@@allow('read'\|'update', id == auth().applicationId \|\| auth().id in job.teamIds)`. `$setAuth(null).application.create({…})` → `AccessDeniedError "Application.create" requires the capability "Application.create" and this caller holds no such grant`. **Why the model is on the grid at all**: a candidate (a bearer, level 0) must be able to WITHDRAW their own application and make no other move. A move's `@gate` only raises the update level ([FJS-1245](#fjs-1245)) and a policy cannot name a move, so the grid is the one spelling — and it works: a bearer holding `['Application.withdraw']` withdraws, the same bearer is refused `reject` by name, a bearer holding nothing is refused both. So *anybody may apply* and *the applicant may only withdraw* are both the client's rules, on one model, and cannot be declared together. **The ruling assumed the other case**: [`FJS-D146`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d146) keeps the gate as *you must be a member at all, which is the one thing a capability cannot say, because a stranger holds no list* — a stranger kept out by the gate. A drop box ([FJS-1244](#fjs-1244)) is the model a stranger is let INTO. **The workaround is invisible in the seed**: the app's principal resolver hands every anonymous caller a baseline `['Candidate.create', 'Application.create', …]`, so which strangers may create what lives in TypeScript. | <a id="fjs-1466"></a>FJS-1466 | litestone · junction | **`@@fts` has no way to say *match without the marks*. None of its tokenizers folds Greek accents or Hebrew points, and `tokenize:` takes a name and nothing else. So the fold is app code on both sides, a loader that writes a shadow column and a service that folds the query, and the declared `$search` directive, which does neither, answers `βίβλος` as typed with 0 rows.** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *remnant, the legacy-port stressor*, Q3, on the real corpus (31,100 verses, 443,808 words). **Every existing route, one at a time:** (1) the four tokenizers: 0 of 6 probe words (`γενεσεως βιβλος Ιησου בראשית ברא אלהים`) against the text as written. Under the default `unicode61`, Hebrew points are separators, so `בְּרֵאשִׁ֖ית` indexes as the fragments `ב`, `אש`, `ית`. (2) A tokenizer argument: `tokenize: "unicode61 remove_diacritics 2"` is refused at parse (`Expected IDENT`), and it would not help: over NFD text in plain FTS5 it folds 2 of 6, because FTS5's diacritic table stops at U+0331, so the perispomeni (U+0342), the iota subscript (U+0345) and every Hebrew point and accent (U+0591–U+05C7) survive. (3) **The nearest declaration that exists**: `function fold(s: String): String { @@expr("replace(…)") }` plus `textFolded String @fold(text)`. It takes 199 nested `replace()`s (the 147 precomposed letters and 52 marks this corpus holds, 3.7 KB of SQL), and it agrees with the JS fold on all 443,808 words and hits 6 of 6. But it writes in 24.1 s against 8.8 s for a JS-folded column (9.0 s for `@@fts` over the raw text), it is keyed to this dataset's characters (a data release bringing one more is silently unfolded), and the query side still needs a JS twin: a schema function is inlined into DDL, not a callable SQL function, and bun:sqlite registers none. **What remnant shipped** ([fold.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/src/core/fold.ts): NFD, strip `\p{M}`, drop transliteration's syllable dots): five shadow columns written by the loader, and the query folded in [search.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/src/services/search.service.ts). Over HTTP every probe and all four of Q3's queries (`G0976`, `βίβλος`, `biblos`, `record`) find Matthew 1:1, median 1–8 ms. **What it costs:** nothing ties the shadow to its source, so a write of `text` that forgets `textFolded` leaves the index answering the old text. Adding it to a loaded corpus took a 46 s refold through the client, because SQL cannot compute the fold, while the same fold at load time is free. And **every other entrance misses**: `GET /api/verses?$search=βίβλος` → 0 rows, `biblos` → 0, and only a pre-folded `βιβλος` → Matthew 1:1. **And it is not only accents**: the corpus writes ί as U+1F77 in all 2,138 lemmas that carry it, while a Greek keyboard types U+03AF, which is canonically the same letter in different bytes. So an unnormalized index misses even a correctly accented query. **Candidate**: a word on the index, `@@fts([text], fold: marks)` (NFD, strip marks), with litestone keeping the shadow on every client write, as it already stamps `@default(ulid())` in JS, and folding the query inside `search()`. Then `$search`, a Resource's search and a console all agree. Measured for Greek and Hebrew only; by FTS5's own table the same holds for any mark above U+0331 (read, not measured) · [remnant PLAN.md Q3](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/PLAN.md) · [remnant search.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/test/search.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1464"></a>FJS-1464 | litestone | **`contains`, `startsWith` and `endsWith` pass the caller's `%` and `_` to `LIKE` as wildcards. `contains: '_'` matches all 31,100 verses, none of which holds an underscore, and `startsWith: 'j_'` matches 4,842.** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *remnant, the legacy-port stressor*, Phase 2, through the client (`$setAuth(null).verse.count`) on the loaded corpus: `english contains "_"` → 31,100 (a scan of the rows finds 0 holding `_`), `contains "%"` → 31,100, `startsWith "%"` → 31,100, `reference startsWith "j_"` → 4,842 (every John, Jude, Judges, Joshua, Job…; a literal `j_` prefix matches 0), `contains "gene%logy"` → 10, the same as `genealogy`. **Cause**: [query.js:514](packages/litestone/src/core/query.js) (and the JSON-path twin at :795) builds `params.push(\`%${operand}%\`)` with no `ESCAPE` clause and no escaping of `%`, `_` or `\`. **Why it matters**: `contains` is what a search box reaches when a model has no `@@fts`, and what a filter form's text field sends, so a person's `50%`, `snake_case` or `C_1` answers rows that don't hold it, and the result looks plausible. It also hides the port trap the other way: the old FJS line added the `%` by hand (`contains: '%' + term + '%'`, remnant's old Search), and today that doubles harmlessly (10 = 10), so a porter never learns the operator already wraps. **Only the client layer was measured**; the HTTP filter reaches the same builder by reading, not by a request. Fix: escape `\` `%` `_` in the operand and emit `LIKE ? ESCAPE '\'`, the one place for all three operators; a caller who wants a pattern has `$raw` · [remnant PLAN.md Q3](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/PLAN.md) |

| <a id="fjs-1442"></a>FJS-1442 | sierra | **`goto()` drops a `null` filter from the URL — `buildUrl` keeps `v !== null` — so `list.apply({ collectionId: null })` (*the rows with no collection*) navigates to the unfiltered list, while toolbelt's encoder writes `null` and the parser reads `?collectionId=null` back as `null`** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28 in Chrome on *Portal, the search stressor*'s Bookmarks page (PLAN.md § Bookmarks): the Unsorted link called `list.apply({ collectionId: null }, directives)` and landed on `/bookmarks/?$limit=60&$orderBy=-createdAt`, All bookmarks. `router/match.js` `buildUrl` filters `undefined`, `null` and `''` out of the params before `encodeQueryString`, whose own module says (`toolbelt/src/query/query.js`) that *null is a filter, and dropping it was a call that answered every row*. | <a id="fjs-1444"></a>FJS-1444 | sierra · ui | **Two lists of one resource on one page share its store, so a page cannot show a filtered list beside the same model's counts or a second filter; *Portal, the search stressor*'s Bookmarks sidebar took its per-collection counts from a schema `@from` instead, and its search page reads ONE list of every bookmark (capped at 500) for both the quicklink tiles and the *already kept* mark** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Read, then built around** 2026-09-28 (PLAN.md § Bookmarks): `list.js` seeds and subscribes a non-composed list to `resource.store`, and a load replaces the store, so a second `bookmarks.list({ state: 'local', query: { quicklink: true } })` on the page that shows `bookmarks.list()` would have each overwrite the other's rows. The same sequence against a list built in the page console (`.probe/phase3/list-reload.ts`) is correct every time (0 → 1 → 2 → 3, `save()` or `service.create`, immediate or delayed reload). Changing only the declaration to `let files = null` / `files = documents.list(…)` fixes it. Mesa makes a top-level `const` that reads a prop DERIVED, so the likely mechanism is this row's: the list is re-derived into a second list over the same store, and one overwrites the other. The mechanism is inferred, not traced; the symptom and the one-line fix are measured. | <a id="fjs-1424"></a>FJS-1424 | litestone · junction | **Nothing records *this answer used Joe's data* at any grain but one entry per read, attributed and clocked. Three searches by me, through `usages.prepare` over HTTP: one about golf (Joe's lens, shared with me, applies), one about carbonara, one about no topic. **Every search**, 3 of 3, wrote a `lens` and a `domain_rule` read entry with `actorId` me, `createdAt`, `source: usages.prepare` and the request's `correlationId`. **Joe's lens id was named on all three**, because matching a lens by its keywords reads every lens the searcher may read. **No rule id was named** on any of them ([FJS-1422](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1422)). So as a record of USE it says no more than the grant already does (Joe's lens was readable). What it adds is *when I searched*, which is what Portal's `Usage` is `@@anonymous` to keep off the trail (Phase 3). Through [FJS-1413](#fjs-1413) that clock joins to the query text in every provider's log. `db.$audit()` has the same grain (actor, clock, correlation id), and FJS-D349's check refuses it only beside an anonymous write inside one transaction. **What Portal records instead:** `Grant`'s `@@log(audit)`, meaning who COULD read whose lens and from when to when (actor Joe, grantee, lens, `transition: 'revoke'`, measured), and the answer itself names each lens that applied, with its owner, to the one person it served (terminal footer and page). **The spelling wanted, not coined:** a record of use at the DATA OWNER's grain, not the reader's (*Joe's golf lens was used N times this month*: no actor, no clock). Today that is an app-written `@@anonymous` counter like Portal's `Usage`. With a single grantee even that count is the grantee's topic frequency, which is why this is a question of grain and not an attribute. | <a id="fjs-1423"></a>FJS-1423 | mesa | **`bind:value={pick[l.id]}` (a bind to a MEMBER of a `let`) compiles clean and writes the member in place, so nothing else that reads the `let` re-renders. AGENTS.md lists `obj.n = 2` as a silent failure and says to follow the mutation with `obj = obj`, but a bind's setter is written by the compiler, so the author has nowhere to put that line.** **Measured** 2026-09-27, *Portal, the search stressor* Phase 5, in Chrome: Joe's Lenses page, one `<select>` per lens bound to `pick[l.id]`. Picking *Me* set the element's value, and the Share button stayed `disabled: true`. Clicking it did nothing, with no console error, exception or failed request. Compiled: `$$runtime.bindInput($$el, 'value', () => ($$runtime.get($$sig_pick)[l().id]), ($$v) => { $$runtime.get($$sig_pick)[l().id] = $$v; })`, with no `set`, against `bind:value={one}` → `$$set_one`. Fix: the compiler owns this setter, so for a member target it can emit the write followed by `$$set_pick($$runtime.get($$sig_pick))`, which is the `obj = obj` the docs ask for. | <a id="fjs-1420"></a>FJS-1420 | junction · sierra | **`FJS-D13` sends a stream of records to a channel, *where the pipeline produced each frame*, and a channel cannot carry ONE call's progressive answer. `publish()` runs once per call. And a live store refuses a frame that is not a row. So the only spelling that paints a result as it arrives, into a live store and through the pipeline, is to write the result as rows, which for *Portal, the search stressor* is keeping the query.** **Measured** 2026-09-27 (PLAN.md Q2), five fake providers (40 ms, 40 ms, 600 ms, flaky, dead), the page painting each as it lands. Three spellings built and driven at the API (10 rounds) and in Chrome (5 rounds). **A**, a raw route over `ctx.sse()`, with the metered half through a service call: first hit ≈ 50 ms. It streams only after working around `FJS-1416`. It skips the gate, hooks, directives and the envelope, and it is HTTP only, beside the page's open socket. No browser package reads `text/event-stream`, and `EventSource` cannot carry a bearer token, so the browser half is fetch, a reader and the frame format by hand (≈ 40 lines). It is the one spelling that reaches only the connection that asked, and the one that hears a hang-up (`onDisconnect`). **B**, each merged hit a `SearchHit` row in a live `list()`: first hit ≈ 60 ms, 46 announcements and 20 rows per search, each row holding the query in its title and URL, on disk until swept (`@@expires` schedules nothing). Every live `Usage` store refuses every frame with that warning, so a non-row payload can land in no store. **The fork, written down:** stream outside the pipeline (A), or write to stream (B). C is neither: its frames leave the pipeline after the first. The same ask from another side as `FJS-1403`, `FJS-1410` and `FJS-1412`: a method's answer has no declaration, so it cannot say it arrives in parts or that it must not be kept. Candidate shapes, not coined: a method that answers in frames over the calling connection, each through `protect()`, with the client exposing them to a store as a sequence of one result; or a connection on the service context, so an after-return frame can at least be addressed | open | 2026-09-30 | [portal PLAN.md Q2](../fjs-prototypes/portal/PLAN.md) · [portal fakes/progressive.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/fakes/progressive.ts) · [portal fakes/drive-web.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/fakes/drive-web.ts) · [DECISIONS.md FJS-D13](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d13) · `FJS-1416` · `FJS-1419` · `FJS-1403` · `FJS-1410` · `FJS-706` · The reader half of spelling A is closed by FJS-1581: readEvents in @frontierjs/sierra/fetch, over toolbelt's /sse, which ctx.sse() writes with; portal's streamA now reads through it. | <a id="fjs-1409"></a>FJS-1409 | conduit | **A request's own `timeout_ms` is graded as the target's fault, so one impatient caller opens a healthy target's breaker for every caller of that conduit.** `timeout` is in `TARGET_FAULTS` whichever timer produced it: the target's declared `timeout_ms`, or a shorter one the CALLER put on the request. **Measured** 2026-09-27 in *Portal, the search stressor*. A target declares no timeout, so its 600 ms answer is fine by its own policy. Five sends with `timeout_ms: 100` give five `timeout`s, and the next send, with `timeout_ms: 2000`, answers `circuit_open` (control: the same send on a fresh conduit answered in 600 ms). In the app, a 400 ms search budget sheds the `slow` provider after five queries, and a page that would wait two seconds for it (Phase 4's progressive results) is told `circuit_open … retrying in 24s`. The terminal names a healthy provider *circuit_open*. For the 400 ms caller the skip is what it wants, because a request it will always abandon is spend with no answer, and that is exactly why the skip belongs to that caller: the breaker is per target, the patience is per caller, and conduit cannot tell them apart. Candidate: a timeout shorter than the target's own counts as `other` (trips nothing, clears nothing), or the breaker keys on the target and the caller's timeout together. **Measured beside it, and right**: the breaker does count a budget miss, so `dead` is asked 5 times in 50 and skipped from the sixth, and `flaky` at 100 % likewise; at 30 % it never trips (15 misses in 50, never five in a row). A 20-query burst asks `dead` 20 times before the first fault lands, since the breaker counts completed faults; that is ordinary breaker behavior, bounded by `max_concurrent` **Phase 4, on the page (2026-09-27)**: the progressive page waits 2 s per provider (every target's ceiling) and the terminal sends its 400 ms as each request's `timeout_ms`, over one shared conduit. Driven in Chrome: six terminal searches, then one on the page. The page painted `charlie · circuit_open`, though `charlie` answers every request in 600 ms and the page would have waited for it. So the caller that FJS-1409 predicted is real: the person who chose the patient surface loses a healthy provider because another surface was impatient **Beside it, 2026-09-30:** `FJS-1408` gave the caller a way to be impatient that the breaker does not count, `signal` (a miss answers `aborted`), and Portal's terminal budget now uses it. `req.timeout_ms` still trips the breaker as measured, and that is this row | open | 2026-09-27 | [portal api/test/fanout.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/test/fanout.test.ts) (pinned) · [portal fakes/bench.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/fakes/bench.ts) · [resilience.ts `TARGET_FAULTS`](packages/conduit/src/resilience.ts) · [conduit.ts `send`](packages/conduit/src/conduit.ts) |
