| <a id="fjs-1567"></a>FJS-1567 | portal | **Portal, the search stressor: page capture does not follow a <meta http-equiv=refresh> redirect, and a capture that reads 0 words is still status ok, so a redirect stub or a client-rendered page is kept as an empty copy with nothing to say so** | open | 2026-09-29 | Found seeding the knight persona (2026-09-29). https://www.conventionalcommits.org/ answers 200 with a 208-byte page whose only content is a meta refresh to /en/; https://www.inkandswitch.com/local-first/ answers 200 with 152 bytes refreshing to /essay/local-first. [fetch.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/pages/fetch.ts) follows HTTP redirects only (redirect: 'manual', each hop re-checked by the SSRF guard), and nothing in api/src/domain/pages reads a refresh. Both were kept as status ok, words 0, finalUrl the stub. A client-rendered page does the same: https://chromedevtools.github.io/devtools-protocol/ (content in a <template>, filled by script) is ok with 0 words; not a parser defect, but indistinguishable from one on the page. [capture.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/pages/capture.ts) writes status ok for any parse, and capture.ts:71 (hasCopy = words != null) then treats the empty copy as kept, so a link check never retries it. Fix: follow a same-document meta refresh (delay 0, http(s) only) as one more guarded hop; and write a 0-word parse as failed (for example 'the page had no readable text'), keeping link live, so the page shows the capture failed and offers Capture again. Seen with FJS-1566, whose empty copies are the same symptom from a different cause |
| <a id="fjs-1564"></a>FJS-1564 | repo · cli · css · sierra · vscode | **No surface or realm has a color of its own, so nothing a developer sees says at a glance which part of the app it came from** | open | 2026-09-29 | The goal: pick one primary color per realm (Data, API, UI, plus Deployment and Testing) and per surface (api, web, site, widgets, extension, desktop, cli), and carry it everywhere a dev sees that part of the app until the color alone says where they are. Today the nearest thing is accidental: `fli dev` prefixes come from bun run --parallel ([new.md:265](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md#L265)), which colors by script ORDER, so dev:web is yellow in one app and cyan in the next. **Where it would help:** (1) fli dev log prefixes and its banner (slot · web 8003 · API 8103), each surface and port in its color; (2) every realm-tagged log line -- litestone, caravan, junction $.log -- so a scrolling terminal sorts itself; (3) fli ci phases, fli proves / prove, fli check findings, fli next and fli ws:atlas, tagged by realm or surface; (4) fli new and fli make:* output naming what they created; (5) a FrameworkError's terminal print naming the realm it was thrown in; (6) the browser tab in dev -- a tinted favicon per surface so web and site tabs differ, the Sierra error overlay border, the dev banner; (7) Litestone Studio chrome in the Data color, Junction devtools in API, each registerDevService entry in its own; (8) the VS Code extension -- file icons for .lite (Data) and .mesa (UI), folder colors for api/ web/ site/ db/, the status bar; (9) docs -- ARCHITECT.md's realm diagram, README § Project Structure, VOCABULARY entries, the docs site, the data-/api-/ui-hazards skill headers, audit artifacts; (10) Basecamp and Outpost -- an app's surfaces in their colors across servers, deploys and releases; (11) test output -- the Suite realm, and which drive (API tier or browser) a failure came from; (12) the ISSUES.md Pkg column as fli next and register views render it. **Constraints:** one owner for the table (surface → realm, hue, ANSI and hex), most likely toolbelt since cli, css, sierra and the vscode extension all read it -- or beside CAT in [ports.js](packages/cli/core/ports.js), already one row per surface -- never a copy per package (Invariant 4); in @frontierjs/css it is a tone for DEV chrome only and never reaches a shipped app's styling (Invariant 13); color is never the only signal -- a label or glyph goes with it, NO_COLOR and 16-color terminals degrade cleanly, and the palette is checked for color-blind separation; "surface" already means a background layer in @frontierjs/css (surface.css), so the naming must not add a third sense. **Open questions (decision-rules):** realm as hue family and surface as a shade inside it (web, site, widgets, extension and desktop are all UI) or a distinct hue per surface; what color cli gets; whether Deployment and Testing get colors or stay neutral |

| <a id="fjs-1504"></a>FJS-1504 | litestone | **A `File` column on a local store with no `publicBase` reads as `null` through every client, the system's included, while a create answers the raw ref with its storage key: `file.js` says *"use asSystem() to get the raw ref"*, and asSystem gets `null`. | <a id="fjs-1498"></a>FJS-1498 | litestone | **A counter keyed by a unique pair has no one-statement spelling: `upsert` refuses `{ increment }` in its update half (FJS-D27 put the operators on `update`/`updateMany` only), so `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET n = n + 1`, the write every aggregate table is made of, is three statements and a caught conflict in app code.** | open | 2026-09-29 | **Measured** 2026-09-29, *jazzhr stressor* Phase 2: spelling A of Q1 is an `IntakeCount { source, quarter, count }` bumped on every application. `upsert({ where: { source, quarter }, create: { …, count: 1 }, update: { count: { increment: 1 } } })` is refused: *"increment" changes a value that is already there, so it belongs on update, not upsert*. The refusal is deliberate (the method's fast path reads `excluded`, the slow path calls `update()`, and only one could apply an operator), and the message names no way out. What the app wrote instead ([pipeline.ts `bumpIntake`](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/api/src/lib/pipeline.ts)): `updateMany` with the operator; if the count is 0, `create`; if that throws on the unique pair, `updateMany` again. Correct under concurrency only because of the retry, and three round trips for one fact. Also met on the way, and a GOOD refusal: an operator on a column with `@gte` is refused with *state the bound as `@check`*. Candidate: the fast path emits `SET "count" = "count" + excluded…` for an operator, and the slow path is refused for an operator instead of the whole method, or the message names the three-statement shape · [FJS-D27](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d27) · [pipeline.ts](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/api/src/lib/pipeline.ts) |

After adding `@system` to a column, `fli db:jsonschema` updated `db/.json/schema.json` (`number` now `readOnly`, out of `required`) and `db/schema.json` still said `required: ['teamId', 'number', …]` — a reader of the wrong one sees a rule the schema no longer states. `db/migrate.md` runs `litestone jsonschema --schema ${schema}` with no `--out`; `db/push.md` resolves `jsonSchemaPath()` from `core/derived-paths.js` and says why: *the dot-directory is this repo's mark for a derived document … and it is gitignored for the same reason* — but `makeGitignore()` has no `.json/` line (the same list [`FJS-1297`](#fjs-1297) is about). Worth: `migrate` takes the same `jsonSchemaPath()`, and `makeGitignore()` gains `db/.json/`. · [migrate.md](packages/cli/commands/db/migrate.md) · [push.md](packages/cli/commands/db/push.md) · [new.md `makeGitignore`](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md) |

The same scaffold writes `database audit { path env("AUDIT_PATH", "./db/audit/") driver logger retention 90d }` and `AUDIT_PATH=./db/audit/`, and a logger-driver database is a DIRECTORY of `auditLogs.jsonl` plus its `.index.db` — none of which the four globs match. Measured in linear: after the first boot `git status` shows `db/audit/` untracked. connectteam added the line by hand and did not file it; linear added it with a comment. One line in `makeGitignore()`. **Amended 2026-09-28, *remnant, the legacy-port stressor* (third app; it was briefly re-filed as FJS-1461, closed as this row): it holds more than who wrote what.** After remnant's first sign-ins the log held 44 entries, including `login.failed` rows, which record the attempted address on purpose (auth.ts: *the only identifier a failed attempt for an unknown user has*), and `support.started` with its reason. So the first `git add .` commits the addresses strangers typed at the login form, which argues S3 rather than S4. Also: `fli check` could compare every declared `database` path against `.gitignore`, which would have caught all three apps |
| <a id="fjs-1295"></a>FJS-1295 | litestone | **`createClient` can still throw `SQLITE_BUSY` from `PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL` when several processes open one new file at once, so a writer dies at boot.** `test/jsonl-multiprocess.test.ts` › *no writer is killed and no row is dropped* failed 1 of 5 runs **in isolation** on 2026-09-22 (and once in the full suite), with `SQLiteError: database is locked` (plain `SQLITE_BUSY`, errno 5) out of `openSqliteConnections` for a child's `main.db`, before any write. Separate from [`FJS-729`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-729), which names the other test in the same file and never reproduced alone | open | 2026-09-22 | Undiagnosed. First question is whether the first open of a new file needs the WAL switch retried around the busy handler, since SQLite skips the handler where waiting could deadlock · [client.js `openSqliteConnections`](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) |

| <a id="fjs-1292"></a>FJS-1292 | litestone | **`litestone advise` tells an app to put `@type` on its `@@extensible` column, which the parser refuses, and on the `fieldsSlots` mirror that `@@extensible(max:)` generated.** | open | 2026-09-22 | Found in linear's Phase 1. The undescribed-Json rule lists `Issue.fields` and `Issue.fieldsSlots` as *possible: an undescribed Json column … @type(Name) binds it, which validates the write and generates a form*. For `fields`, following the advice is a parse error, by name: *'fields' also carries @type, which closes it to the keys this file names* (the `@@extensible` block in [parser.js](packages/litestone/src/core/parser.js)). The app never wrote `fieldsSlots`: the framework generated it. The rule should skip an `@@extensible` column and any column the framework generates. | <a id="fjs-1287"></a>FJS-1287 | cli | **The scaffolded README still says `bun run dev` serves *the api on 8100, the web app on 8000*. The template in [new.md](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md) (`'the api on 8100'`, `'the web app on 8000'`, and the fixed formula `env*1000 + category*100 + project*10 + service`) was never updated when [FJS-1148](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1148) moved slots into `fli dev`. **Measured on one machine, with another app's vite on 8000:** `fli dev --dry` answers *slot 2, web 8002 · API 8102*, and `fli dev` binds exactly those (`ss -ltnp`). The README's `bun run dev` starts the API on 8100, and then vite dies with *Port 8000 is already in use* (`strictPort`), which takes the API down with it. It fails loudly, so it costs minutes, not data. The fix is the README naming `fli dev` and saying the slot is per root (`fli ps` shows it). |

'debug' : 'production'`, and `config.debug` is set only by `DEBUG=1` ([config/index.ts](packages/junction/src/config/index.ts)), while the environment that `loadConfig` layers on is `NODE_ENV`. **Measured:** a fresh `fli new --full` app with `.env` `NODE_ENV=development`, started by `fli dev` (`bun --watch`), logs `devtools=disabled mode=production`. The field answers *is debug on*, but its label claims the environment, so a developer looking at why a dev server behaves like a production one is told that it is one. Either name the field `debug=` or report `NODE_ENV`. |
| <a id="fjs-1282"></a>FJS-1282 | cli | **`fli` cites an Invariant BY NUMBER in output an app developer reads, and the numbered list lives in this repo's root `CLAUDE.md`, which no installed app has — so the reason for a refusal is a pointer to nothing.** | open | 2026-09-22 | `fli check` prints `(invariant 18)` beside a failed rule ([checks.js](packages/cli/core/checks.js) `rule.invariant`, and the `--list` column in [check.md](packages/cli/commands/fli/check.md)); `fli make:widget` refuses a name with *(Invariant 19)* ([widget.md](packages/cli/commands/make/widget.md)); the adopt lesson says *(Invariant 2)* ([03-introspect.md](packages/cli/commands/tutor/_steps-adopt/03-introspect.md)); and the help prose of `make:resource`, `web:resource` and `make:site` does the same. The word is contributor vocabulary ([Invariant](VOCABULARY.md)) and the number is only meaningful beside the list. The fix is to state the RULE in the message — *model names are PascalCase singular* — and keep the number where the reader has the list: comments, registers, `invariants.snapshot.md`. `rule.invariant` stays on the rule table, since `ws:invariants` reads it · [VOCABULARY.md](VOCABULARY.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1268"></a>FJS-1268 | cli · notifications | **`fli check`'s `surface-src` grades FILES at a surface root and never directories, so a whole directory of application source sitting beside `src/` is unreported — and it is the location `@frontierjs/notifications` probes FIRST.** | open | 2026-09-22 | Found wiring reminders on connectteam Phase 6. Invariant 3 is *a surface keeps its source in `src/`, and only its entry beside it*, and the rule enforces it: a stray `api/probe.ts` written during this session was reported on the next run, by name, with the reason. `resolveNotificationsDir` probes `[resolve(base, 'notifications'), resolve(base, 'src', 'notifications')]` and takes the first that exists ([loader.ts:56](packages/notifications/loader.ts)), where `base` is the directory of the entry — so for an app whose entry is `api/index.ts` the preferred location is `api/notifications/`, a directory of app source beside `api/src/`. **Measured:** `api/notifications/ShiftReminder.notification.ts` in place, `fli check --only surface-src` → *nothing to report*. The same file one directory further in is where `example/` puts it and is what this app declares. Two small things, either of which closes it: have the rule walk directories at a surface root as well as files, and/or reverse the package's probe order so the layout it prefers is the one the invariant wants. The probe order matters on its own — an app that lets itself be probed rather than declaring `notifications:` gets whichever it happens to have created. · [loader.ts](packages/notifications/loader.ts) · Invariant 3 |
| <a id="fjs-1252"></a>FJS-1252 | cli | **`fli check`'s `service-model` fires on the spelling junction's own AGENTS.md teaches, and the message asserts a security consequence the running app contradicts.** | open | 2026-09-22 | The rule says a hyphenated service file *singularizes to 'clock-entry', while `db.<accessor>` is the model name with a lower first letter, so a hyphenated name never meets it. The miss is silent and fails open: the `@@gate` is not found and the model is served to anyone.* Junction's AGENTS.md says the opposite in as many words — *`order-lines.service.ts` becomes the service `orderLines` over `OrderLine`* — and `example/` ships ten hyphenated services. **Measured**, connectteam Phase 5, with `model:` deliberately absent: `kiosk-devices.service.ts` served `/api/kioskDevices`, and `KioskDevice @@gate("6.6.6.6")` answered **403 to an ADMINISTRATOR(5)** and 401 to a stranger — the gate was found and enforced, so *fails open* is not what happens. The advice is still right (`example` states `model:` on every one, and this app now does too), but a rule whose message describes a hole that is not there costs the reader a measurement to find out, and this is the rule most likely to be met on the first day of a new surface · [checks.js § service-model](packages/cli/core/checks.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1255"></a>FJS-1255 | junction · sierra | **A declared MOVE and a service METHOD are separate namespaces, so a screen that derives its buttons from `@@transitions` — which is what sierra offers it — can only call a method that happens to share the name, and gets a 405 when it does not.** | open | 2026-09-22 | **Driven in a browser**, connectteam Phase 5. `resource.transitions(row, level)` reads `@@transitions` off the registered schema and answers `{ name, from, to, gate, allowed }`, which is exactly the right thing for a queue screen: the button set comes from the schema, a published shift stops offering *publish*, and a per-move gate disables what the caller cannot do. The screen then calls `resource.service.invoke(name, id)` — there is nothing else to call — and the move `export: approved -> exported @gate(8)` met a service method named `exportToPayroll`, so the button answered `Service 'timesheets' does not offer 'export' (allowed: find, get, patch, submit, approve, reopen, build, exportToPayroll)`. Renaming the method fixed it, which is the whole finding: the two names are joined by nothing but an author remembering. `fli check`'s `transition-methods` grades the other direction only — *a declared move and the code that makes it still name each other* — and was **green** on this app while the button was 405ing, because a method named `exportToPayroll` does name the move in its body. Either the rule should also ask whether every declared move is reachable BY ITS OWN NAME, or a service should be able to declare `{ method: 'exportToPayroll', transition: 'export' }` and have the client resolve it **Re-measured 2026-09-22, linear Phase 3, against states that are ROWS:** there is no name at all. `issue.transitions(row)` answers `[]`, `issue.transition(id, …)` refuses, the JSON Schema has no `x-transitions`, and `transition-methods` reports *no @@transitions in db/schema.lite*, so the rule is green because it has nothing to read. The moves ARE readable, as `WorkflowTransition` rows under the team's own policy (hidden with a private team, measured), so a screen can draw its buttons from data and move with a plain `patch({ stateId })`. [`FJS-D365`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-d365) B would give each move a name (its target's `@@label`) and bring `transitions()` back · [workflows.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/linear/api/test/workflows.test.ts) · [timesheets.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/services/timesheets.service.ts) · [approvals/index.mesa](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/web/src/routes/approvals/index.mesa) |
| <a id="fjs-1256"></a>FJS-1256 | junction | **A custom method has ONE answer, and it is both the caller's result and the payload broadcast to every other tab — so a write whose useful answer is a report makes every connected client log *ignored an update with no id* and drop it.** | open | 2026-09-22 | **Driven in a browser**, connectteam Phase 5. `timesheets.build` writes a `Timesheet` and one `TimesheetLine` per measured clock entry, and what the caller needs back is the report — how many lines, which clocks were still running, which days had no pay rate — so it answers `{ sheet, lines, stillRunning, noRate }`. That answer is announced on the service's channel under the method's own name, and junction's client logged `[Junction] ignored an update with no id — it cannot be matched to a row. An announcement carries the record it is about.` The documented instrument is `$.dispatch = false`, which is a boolean: the app can announce a shape no client can use, or announce nothing and leave every other tab unaware that a timesheet now exists. There is no third spelling — `$.dispatch` cannot name the row to announce, and returning the row instead loses the report the caller came for. The row's own guidance names the read-shaped case (*a count, a report, a credential* → `$.dispatch = false`); this is the write-shaped one, where silence has a cost. A `$.announce(row)` beside `$.dispatch`, or a method-level `announces:` naming which part of the answer is the record, would close it · [timesheets.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/services/timesheets.service.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1262"></a>FJS-1262 | junction | **A custom method whose payload is legitimately empty cannot be called over HTTP: `{}` is read as *no body* and answered 400, while the same call over the WebSocket works — so the transport the browser prefers hides it.** | open | 2026-09-22 | **Measured**, connectteam Phase 5. `POST /api/clockEntries` with `X-Service-Method: stop` and body `{}` → `400 {"name":"BadRequest","message":"Request body is required"}`; the same request with `{"shiftId":null}` → `200` and the row; no body at all → the same 400. The browser never sees it, because the client prefers the socket once connected and the WS path carries `{}` happily — driven: the *Clock out* button on the employee screen works in a tab and the identical call from `curl` does not. Every non-browser caller is on the HTTP path: a script, a webhook, a native shell, a server-to-server call, and `fli`-driven smoke tests. *Clock me out*, *revoke my other sessions*, *mark all read* are ordinary methods whose payload is nothing, and the workaround is to invent a key · [body.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/body.ts) · [clock-entries.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/services/clock-entries.service.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1234"></a>FJS-1234 | litestone · caravan | **A predicate that depends on a ZONE held in a column cannot be a `where`, so deferred work over per-row wall-clock times is a full scan of the window: every candidate row is read and all but a few are discarded in application code.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Found building reminders on the calendly stressor (PLAN.md § Q9). The product rule is *08:00 on the morning of, in the INVITEE's zone* — the one people actually want, as against *24 hours before*, which is easy precisely because a duration needs no zone. **The query that should exist cannot be written.** `booking.inviteeTimeZone` is a column, so *has 08:00 passed for this row* is a calendar calculation per row against a different zone per row. `where` has no zone-aware operator and no index can serve one, so the sweep bounds what it can — `startsAt` between now and now+48h, an ordinary instant comparison — and asks the real question in JavaScript. **Measured on a small database: `considered 60, sent 2`.** The ratio is set by the horizon and the spread of zones, not by how many reminders are due, so it does not improve with scale — it is 2 sends from 60 reads today and 2 from 60,000 on a real calendar. **Re-measured at workforce scale on connectteam 2026-09-22, and the claim holds with one correction.** 4,000 published shifts over a fortnight, 600 people, 8 sites, a 48-hour horizon, a five-minute cron. ONE fire at 07:00Z — 08:00 where half the workforce is, the most productive instant of that day — **considered 566, sent 96**, discarding **264 pending + 206 tooLate** on the predicate that cannot be lowered and **0 past** on the one that can, in **773 ms**. Over a whole day the same window is re-read 288 times for the same 173 reminders the employer owes: **165,744 rows read, 173 sent — 1 send per 958 rows**, and **~180 seconds of CPU a day** to do it (24 fires measured at 621 ms each across the block holding both zones' 08:00; the read total is arithmetic on the measured per-fire figure, the sends are measured). **The correction is which of the two terms matters.** Moving the same 600 people from 2 zones (the brief's: Lisbon and the Azores, ONE hour apart) to 12 spread around the world left `considered` **identical at 566** and moved the sends only from 96 to 81 — spreading people over more zones spreads their 08:00s over the clock rather than changing how many there are. **So the cost is set by the horizon alone, and the spread is second order**: an employer with one hour of spread pays a global calendar's price. That removes the one escape a reader might infer from this row — that a geographically tight product is cheap. **And the cheap fix this row floats does not reach this case.** A `@generated` column holding the reminder instant fails here for a stronger reason than *cannot do arithmetic*: the zone is `User.timeZone`, which is **two relations** from `Shift` (`Shift → Employee → User`), and *which* morning is itself undecided between `Shift.onDate` (the day the manager typed, in the SITE's zone) and `startsAt` read in the person's — two columns the schema does not relate. Measured: the same shift is `tooLate` for a Lisbon employee and `pending` for an Azorean one, with reminder instants a DAY apart. **`tooLate` is a second consequence worth naming**, at 206 of 566 rows in one fire: a rule stated as a wall clock has shifts it can never remind at all — every shift starting before 08:00 local, which in a field workforce is the early turn. A rule stated as a duration has no such case, which is precisely why building that one would have measured nothing. · driven in connectteam `api/test/reminders.test.ts` § 1, § 6 **This is [FJS-1214](#fjs-1214)'s gap in a second place and the consequence is different enough to be its own row.** 1214 is about DECLARATION — a recurring wall-clock window cannot be spelled in `.lite`. This is about QUERY: the same absence of zone awareness means a zoned predicate cannot be pushed down, and the cost lands on background work rather than on a form. The arithmetic exists and is correct — `fromWall`/`partsIn` in toolbelt answered every DST case in `api/test/reminders.test.ts` first time, including a Tokyo booking whose reminder falls on the previous UTC date — so what is missing is not the maths but anywhere to put it that the database can see. **The cron half is not a defect and is worth stating so the row is not read as one.** `handle(…, { cron, timeZone })` binds ONE zone per schedule (measured: `registrations()` returns `timeZone: null` for an unzoned expression), which is right — a schedule is a statement about the app, not about a row. It is *because* that is right that the work has to become a sweep, and the sweep is where the missing predicate bites. **What would close it:** a zone-aware comparison litestone can lower — the same word [FJS-1214](#fjs-1214) asks for, reused in `where` — or, far cheaper and worth considering first, a `@generated` column holding the reminder instant, which fails today for the reason `Booking.endsAt` already documents in this app's schema: a generated column cannot reach across a relation or do arithmetic on one. **What is NOT a finding, measured and cleared:** caravan's dispatch door. `delay` is a duration and the `jobs` table stores `run_at`, an instant, which looked like `FJS-D143`'s category error one layer down — it is not. The conversion is exact (measured: `run_at` landed on the target instant, off by 0 ms), and a `runAt` option does not exist rather than being silently ignored, so TypeScript refuses it at the call site; reaching it needs a deliberate `as any`. The queue contributed durability, one-fire-per-cluster and retries, and none of Q9's difficulty came from it · [caravan/src/cron.ts](packages/caravan/src/cron.ts) · [FJS-1214](#fjs-1214) · [FJS-D143](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d143) |
| <a id="fjs-1235"></a>FJS-1235 | cli | **The scaffolded `dev:api` watcher does not restart when `db/schema.lite` changes, because the schema is read from disk at runtime and is not in the module graph — so editing the schema silently does nothing and the API keeps serving the old one, answering confidently wrong.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Found on the calendly stressor; cost two stalls before the cause was understood, each spent debugging an app that was correct. **Measured, with a control, on one running process:** `PORT=8102 bun --watch run api/index.ts`, then — (1) a real content change to `api/src/core/db.ts` → the app **restarted** (a second boot banner in the log); (2) a real content change to `db/schema.lite`, including adding an actual column to a model → **no restart**, no log line, no warning. The control is the point: the watcher works, and it is not watching the file that defines the data. **Both halves are scaffolded by `fli new` and they disagree.** `api/src/core/db.ts` builds a schema PATH — `fileURLToPath(new URL('../../../db/schema.lite', import.meta.url))` — and hands it to `createClient`, so the schema is opened with `fs` at runtime and never imported. `bun --watch` watches the module graph. A path string is not an import, so the most-edited file in a schema-first framework is the one file the dev loop ignores. **The symptom is the bad kind: silence plus a plausible wrong answer.** A column added to the schema is absent from the running API, so a request writing it fails validation for a field the developer is looking straight at, and a policy edited to open a door stays shut. Nothing says *restart me*; `fli check` is green because the source is fine; and the natural conclusion is that the schema change is wrong. **Same shape as [FJS-1232](#fjs-1232)** — two scaffolded pieces that do not agree, with nothing announcing it — and this app has now hit that shape three times in two days ([FJS-1233](#fjs-1233) is the third). **Fix:** make the dev script watch the schema — `bun --watch --watch-extensions .lite` if it supports one, otherwise `bunx --bun concurrently` or a small chokidar wrapper in the scaffolded script — or, better, have litestone `createClient` register the schema path with the runtime's watcher when one is present, so every app gets it without a script change. Cheapest interim: one line in the scaffolded `AGENTS.md` under *What a generic habit gets wrong here*, since that file already exists to catch exactly this class · [FJS-1232](#fjs-1232) · [FJS-1233](#fjs-1233) |
| <a id="fjs-1232"></a>FJS-1232 | cli · sierra | **`fli new` writes `NODE_ENV=development` into the app's `.env`, and a production Vite build reads it — so `import.meta.env.DEV` is TRUE and `PROD` is FALSE inside the shipped bundle, and every dev-only branch in the app ships to production.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Found on the calendly stressor while giving the `site/` surface an API origin. The fallback was written the ordinary way — `` VITE_API_URL ?? (import.meta.env.DEV ? 'http://localhost:8100' : '') `` — and `bun run build:site` baked **`http://localhost:8100/api`** into the prerendered island. **Measured three times on one file, changing one line:** with `NODE_ENV=development` present the build emits `` Q=`http://localhost:8100/api` ``; with that single line removed from `.env` and nothing else changed, the same build emits `` Q=`/api` ``; switching the branch to `import.meta.env.MODE === 'development'` emits `/api` with the line back in place. **The chain is all scaffolded, and no part of it is the app author's mistake.** `fli new` writes `NODE_ENV=development` into `.env` ([the scaffolded file](packages/cli/)); the scaffolded `build:site` script runs `bun --bun --env-file=../.env vite build`, which puts it in `process.env`; Vite computes `isProduction` from `process.env.NODE_ENV` ahead of the mode, so `vite build` — whose mode IS `production`, and which minifies, tree-shakes and prerenders as production throughout — defines `import.meta.env.DEV` as `true`. **Why this is worse than a wrong constant.** `DEV`/`PROD` is the documented way to write a branch that must not ship, so the failure lands precisely on the code chosen for being safe: dev URLs, verbose logging, mock payment paths, seeded credentials, disabled auth checks. It is silent at every stage — the build succeeds, `fli check` is green, the output looks right, and the bundle is minified so nobody reads it. On this app it would have shipped a public booking page pointing at `localhost:8100`, and the symptom in production is *no times available*, not an error. **Vite's own docs say NODE_ENV and mode are independent and that setting NODE_ENV in `.env` affects the build**, so the behaviour is theirs and correct; the bug is the scaffold pairing that line with a build script that loads it. **Fix, cheapest first:** stop shipping `NODE_ENV=development` in the scaffolded `.env` (it is what `bun run dev` already implies, and the API reads `NODE_ENV` from the real environment); or drop `--env-file` from the build scripts; or have `createSierraViteConfig` set `define: { 'import.meta.env.DEV': mode !== 'production' }` so the flag follows the mode it claims to describe. A `fli check` rule cannot reach this — it is invisible in source and only appears in the emitted bundle — so a build-time assertion in sierra's build (`DEV` true while mode is `production` → warn by name) is the only grader that would have caught it. Pairs with [FJS-1226](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1226): both are a declared value that boots clean and means something other than it says · [site/config/vite.config.js](packages/sierra/) · [FJS-1226](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1226) |
| <a id="fjs-1233"></a>FJS-1233 | sierra | **An island on the prerendered `site/` surface cannot reach the API by the relative path every other surface uses, and nothing says so — not the dev server, not the build, not `fli check`. The page renders "no times available" over an API it never called.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Found by opening the app's own public booking page on the calendly stressor. The island fetched `` `/api/event-types/${id}` ``, which is correct on `web/` and correct from the server, and resolves against the SITE's origin here — a different deployment by design. **Measured:** `GET`/`POST` to `/api/...` on the site dev server returns the SPA-fallback shell, the island reports `Slots unavailable (404)`, and the page then renders *There are no open times in the next two weeks* — a plausible business answer to a question never asked. **The asymmetry is the finding.** `web/config/vite.config.js` is scaffolded WITH an `/api` proxy and a comment explaining that without it requests hit the SPA fallback; `site/config/vite.config.js` is scaffolded with no proxy, no comment and no alternative, and `site/deploy/serve.js` — the `--serve` host for the built output — has no `/api` handling either. So the same line of code works on one surface and fails on the other, in dev AND in production, and the surface that fails is the one whose whole point is being a separate origin. The `widgets/` surface, by contrast, makes this unmissable: it takes `data-api` as an ORIGIN and cannot be written the relative way. Three surfaces, three different contracts, one of them silent. **Adding a proxy to `site/`'s dev config would be the wrong fix** and is worth saying, because it is the obvious one: it would make dev pass and leave the served build broken, which is strictly worse than failing in both. **The right shape is the widget's** — the site is told the API's origin — so what is missing is the scaffolded `VITE_API_URL` (documented in `.env.example`, defaulting to the relative path for the same-origin-behind-one-proxy deployment) and, ideally, a prerender-time check: sierra already walks every island chunk to publish it, so a relative `/api` literal in an island is statically findable and could be reported the way `publishes:` is. **Also missing: `envDir`.** Both surfaces' vite configs leave it at the default, which is the surface folder, while the app has exactly ONE `.env` at its root — so `VITE_SITE_URL` written where every other variable in the app lives reaches nothing and the client silently falls back to a localhost default. Same failure mode as the above, one layer up · [site/config/vite.config.js](packages/sierra/) · [FJS-1232](#fjs-1232) |
| <a id="fjs-1231"></a>FJS-1231 | ui | **`<Select>` given a `value` that matches none of its options renders blank and says nothing — which is indistinguishable from *nothing is chosen*, and is how a wrong handler presents itself.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Reported on the calendly stressor as *selecting a day makes the selection disappear*. **The cause was the app's**, and this row is not a complaint about the contract: `<Select>` and `<Input>` wire `on:change={onchange}` / `oninput?.(e)` straight to the DOM and their headers say *Native event callbacks*, so a handler written as `(v) => Number(v)` receives an Event, `Number(Event)` is `NaN`, and the control then holds a value no `<option>` has. The browser renders that as an empty box. **What is missing is the sentence.** The component knows, at render time, that it was handed a non-null `value` matching none of its normalized options — it has already built `flatOptions` — and it renders blank instead of saying so. Nothing else catches it either: the page compiles, `fli check` is green, and there is no console output, so the only signal is a control that silently discards what you pick. **The kit already holds this exact principle** and applies it one case over. `optionsNote` exists because *an empty list and a list nobody could fetch render identically, and a person [cannot tell]* ([utils.js:260](packages/ui/utils.js)), and `truncationNote` exists for the same reason. A value that matches no option is the third member of that family and the only one with no note. A `console.warn` naming the component, the value and the option count would have turned a blank box into a one-line answer; a visible note is probably wrong here, since the state is usually a bug rather than something to tell an end user about. Worth pairing with [FJS-1230](#fjs-1230), which recommends the same remedy for a different silent discard, and with [FJS-1208](#fjs-1208). Three components in one kit now drop what they were given without a word, which is a house style rather than three bugs · [Select.mesa](packages/ui/components/forms/Select.mesa) · [utils.js:260](packages/ui/utils.js) · [FJS-1230](#fjs-1230) |
| <a id="fjs-1210"></a>FJS-1210 | sierra · litestone | **A model joining the device schema turns a previously-inert foreign key into a real constraint, and hydration has no answer for it: the warm fills tables in DECLARATION order, so one batch can fail whole on a row the next declaration would have written.** | open | 2026-09-20 | Found by declaring `@@sync(field)` on `example`'s `ProductVariant`. `deviceSchema()` keeps the `@@sync` models plus what they reference, so the variant table appeared on the device — and `InventoryMovement`, which the stockroom already hydrates, has a relation to it. Before the policy that column was an inert number with no table behind it; after it the FK is emitted, and hydrating 40 movements against an empty variants table failed the whole batch: `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY`, reported as a `warning:` the screen never shows. **`example` answers it the way an app can** — declare the variants too, and import them FIRST, since `warmOffline()` walks `_declared` in registration order. What is owed is the framework half: the order is currently an app's to get right, with nothing saying so and nothing checking it, and the failure is a silent console warning plus a device that is emptier than it looks. Two shapes to weigh — sort the warm by FK dependency over the device schema (derivable, and it makes the import order stop mattering), or defer foreign keys for the length of a hydration batch (cheaper, and it lets a batch land whose parents arrive later). An `fli check` rule is the third: a device model referenced by another device model and declared by no `offlineQuery` · [offline.js](packages/sierra/src/junction/offline.js) · [local-db.js](packages/sierra/src/junction/local-db.js) · [device-schema.js](packages/litestone/src/device-schema.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1196"></a>FJS-1196 | orion | **Four screens the mockup specifies have no `.mesa` counterpart** — metrics, templates, plugins, and the run filter bar with its preview and review mode. | open | 2026-09-20 | `web/routes/` answers flows, a flow, runs, a run and credentials. `mockup/ui/components/pages.jsx` (3,173 lines) also carries `MetricsPage`, `TemplatesPage`, `PluginsPage`, `ExecFilterBar`, `ExecutionPreview` and `ExecReviewMode`. **The data each needs already exists** — `runs.metrics` is a built method and the plugin registry is `engine/plugins` — so these are screens over answered questions, unlike the canvas ([FJS-1198](#fjs-1198)) which is the expensive one. Low because a host can operate orion without them: the flows and runs screens cover authoring and watching, and metrics is the only one an operator asks for by name. Filed so the mockup's remaining lines are accounted for rather than implied · [pages.jsx](packages/orion/mockup/ui/components/pages.jsx) · [web/routes](packages/orion/web/routes) |
| <a id="fjs-1185"></a>FJS-1185 | litestone | **A `seeAlso` in the catalog is matched on the word alone, and eight words exist at two levels — so 36 of the cross-references in the generated reference point at the wrong entry, silently.** | open | 2026-09-19 | `CATALOG.find(r => r.word === w)` takes the first row carrying the name, so `@@log`'s pointer at `log` resolves to the FIELD `@log`, `@@id`'s at `unique` to `@unique`, `@@capabilities`'s at `allow` to `@allow`. Some of the 36 are right by luck and some are not, and which is which is a per-row judgment about what the author meant — which is why this is filed rather than swept. The MECHANISM is fixed: a `<level>:<word>` entry resolves to that level, used by `@@index`→`@@unique` (litestone CHANGES 2026-09-19), rendered as the bare word by the assistant and as a link by the reference. What is owed is reading the 36 and qualifying the ones that mean the other level. The sharper fix underneath is to make a bare ambiguous name REFUSE at render, which cannot land until the 36 are resolved — that ordering is the whole of this row. Found fixing `@@index`'s under-reported signature · [catalog-reference.js](packages/litestone/src/tools/catalog-reference.js) · [catalog.js](packages/litestone/src/core/catalog.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1178"></a>FJS-1178 | sierra | **A resource's `offlineQuery` is only registered once its MODULE is evaluated, and a route's modules are code-split — so the screen nobody has opened, which is the one the declaration exists for, declares nothing until somebody opens it.** | open | 2026-09-16 | `createResource` runs at import (Invariant 18) and `declareOffline` runs with it, so a boot that imports no resource warms nothing. `example` answers it with one line in `web/src/main.js` — importing the resource after `virtual:sierra`, which is what builds the client it is created against — and that is a real answer rather than a workaround, since WHICH resources are worth the entry chunk is a bundling decision the app owns. What is owed is the framework half being decidable at all: a glob over `src/resources/` is derivable (Invariant 19 already fixes what that directory holds) and `offline:` already exists in `sierra.config.js` for the shell, so `offline: { resources: true }` is the shape. **Two things make it more than a glob**, and they are why it is filed rather than built: `createResource` needs a client, so an eager import must be ordered AFTER `initJunction` or every resource module throws at boot; and every resource so imported lands in the entry chunk, which is exactly the number `FJS-D302` grades · [offline.js](packages/sierra/src/junction/offline.js) · [main.js](example/web/src/main.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1177"></a>FJS-1177 | litestone | **`@@index([<a relation field>])` parses, migrates and builds an index on a string CONSTANT** — SQLite reads a double-quoted identifier that is not a column as a string literal, so `CREATE INDEX "idx_c_other" ON "c" ("other")` succeeds and indexes the same value for every row. | open | 2026-09-16 | Measured 2026-09-16: `parse()` answers valid, `generateDDL` emits the line, `db.exec` applies it clean, and `sqlite_master` holds the index — which is the whole failure, since a declared index that does not exist is the one defect nothing about the app looks different for. The author almost certainly meant the relation's foreign key, so there are two candidate fixes and they are not the same ruling: refuse it at parse the way `@@index` over a `@computed` is refused (a virtual column is the neighbouring case and is already refused), or MAP it to the relation's `fields:` the way `ddl.js` maps the FK itself. `@@unique` over one has the same shape and is worse, being a constraint. Found while building `src/device-schema.js`, whose filter drops such an index rather than carrying it — that drop is the only thing in the tree that currently notices · [parser.js](packages/litestone/src/core/parser.js) · [ddl.js](packages/litestone/src/core/ddl.js) |

| <a id="fjs-1158"></a>FJS-1158 | junction | **The announcement check warns that a write answered a projection when the only missing column is one the caller can never read**: `flowCredentials.create() answered 9 of 7 columns — missing secret`, on every create of a model with an `@encrypted` column below system. | open | 2026-10-04 | `announcementPayload` compares the answered keys against every column `modelColumns` lists, protected ones included, and a non-system client strips `@encrypted`, `@guarded` and `@secret` from every row it reads. So the warning fires on a derived CRUD service that answered the whole row it could, re-reads the row by id (which strips the same column again), and tells the author to answer a column they are not allowed to see. The count reads nine keys against seven columns for a model with ten, which is a second thing wrong in the same sentence and was not investigated. Found by orion's `flowCredentials` service. The fix is to subtract `$protectedFields(accessor)` from the expected set for a non-system client. Stated in [litestone.ts](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) `announcementPayload` · Seen again by Transit Phase 4 (2026-10-03): subscriptions.create() answered 14 of 11 columns — missing target, on every create of Subscription, whose Slack/webhook URL is @encrypted. Same shape: a derived CRUD service answered every column the caller can read. |

Accepting it is not a parse tweak — it means deciding whether `{` interpolates inside one, and the honest answer is that it should NOT, which makes a CDATA section a literal-text escape hatch and therefore a language feature with a rule of its own. That may well be worth having; it is not a diagnostic. **Cost of NOT doing it is one paste and one error message**, which is why it is filed rather than built. | <a id="fjs-747"></a>FJS-747 | junction | **The suite exits 1 with `0 fail` and no test named, roughly once in ten runs.** Seen three times across 2026-09-02/03, always the same shape: `bun run test` reports `1893 pass · 0 fail`, prints no `(fail)` line anywhere, and exits non-zero — so CI would go red naming nothing. **Two things this row used to say are measured wrong** (2026-09-05, bun 1.3.11). *The stated hypothesis*: an unhandled rejection escaping a test cannot be it — bun installs its own `unhandledRejection` listener, so `process.listenerCount('unhandledRejection')` is **1** inside `bun test`, junction's `listenerCount === 0` guard therefore never installs its rejection handler here at all, and an escaped rejection is attributed by bun to whatever test is running as a `(fail)`, which is the opposite of the observed shape. *The proposed instrument*: a preload registering `process.on('unhandledRejection')` **never fires** — confirmed with the preload loading (it printed) and the rejection still going to bun's own reporter. Following the row as written produces an instrument that is silent for the reason it was built. **What is true instead**: `uncaughtException` has no bun listener, so a started app DOES take that one inside a test process, and `crashStop()` turns a stray throw into an async `process.exit(1)` behind `app.stop()` — a race whose result depends on whether bun force-exits first. All 27 junction test files that `start()` also `stop()`, so nothing is left dangling. **What is ruled out and what is left**: a throw landing while a test runs is reported by bun either way (paired, with and without a started app — identical), and one landing after the last test is swallowed silently at exit 0 because bun force-exits before the timer fires. The remaining window is a throw landing BETWEEN files, where no test is in scope to attribute it to. `process.exitCode = 1` reproduces the reported shape EXACTLY (`1 pass · 0 fail`, no `(fail)` line, exit 1) and nothing in `src/` or `test/` writes it, so it is a shape match rather than the cause — a dependency doing it would look the same. **12 consecutive clean runs** on 2026-09-05, so it is still not reproducible on demand | open | 2026-09-05 | [test/](packages/junction/test) · [app.ts `crashStop`](packages/junction/src/core/app.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-610"></a>FJS-610 | example | **A soft decline is never re-presented, so every invoice is charged exactly once and the distinction the code makes between soft and hard buys nothing.** `declineKind` separates *the money may be there tomorrow* from *this card will never work*, and the whole value of the first is that the clock keeps running and the charge is tried again. Nothing tries it again: `subscription-renew` dispatches `invoice-collect` once, and dunning moves STATES — `Invoice`'s two `@@commitment`s lapse at the grace and cancel at the deadline, `recoverIfClear` recovers when the ledger is clean — and dispatches nothing. So a soft-declined invoice sits `issued` until the deadline cancels the subscription, having been presented one time. **Caravan's retry ladder does not cover it and cannot**: a transport failure throws and retries, but a decline is a domain ANSWER returned as a value, which exits the ladder successfully. The fix is a re-presentment with a clock of its own, under `unique` for [`FJS-609`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-609)'s reason, with the cadence a decision rather than a default — a shop that re-presents daily for 21 days is a shop the card networks notice. Not a defect in anything built: every piece is correct on its own, and this is the half the design implies and nobody wrote. `verify:collect` asserts the soft decline leaves the subscription alone, which is true and is not the same as the money being asked for again | open | 2026-08-30 | [schema.lite `Invoice`](example/db/schema.lite) · [billing.ts `declineKind`](example/api/src/domain/billing/billing.ts) |

The affordance itself already exists — `<Button loading>` is a prop the kit ships — so what is missing is the thing that FLIPS it and restores it, and the restore is where the defect lives: an early return or a throw past the reset leaves a row disabled with nothing to re-enable it. **The repo has already chosen the shape once**: `toasts.loading(msg)` answers `{update, dismiss}` — a handle, not an id ([FJS-119](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-119)) — because settling the thing you started is one value, not a lookup. `KOBAMI/my.maid.tech` arrived at the same answer for elements independently (`const release = Element.temporary(el)` → `release("There was an error")`), which is the useful kind of agreement. Open, and deliberately not designed here: whether it is an attachment in `@frontierjs/ui` (`{@attach busy}`), a pure function in toolbelt over a node, or a `<Form>`-shaped context the way `$context.form` already answers labels and errors. The vocabulary was not rejected; it was out-competed by whatever was quicker under deadline, which is what a rule with no check always loses to. `fli check` is the place — it already owns everything derived from the seed while Biome owns generic JavaScript (`FJS-D33`), and it reads `.mesa`, which Biome does not. ---

## Needs a decision

These are questions, not defects. A ruling moves to `DECISIONS.md` and closes the
row here. **This table is the questions with an id; most open questions are argued
inside an IDEAS paper's `## Open questions` and stay there.** `fli decisions` reads
both as one queue, and a question becomes pickable — by `fli decide` or on `fli gui`
— once its bullet carries lettered options and a `**Recommend X**` line. A row's
cell cannot hold options, so they go in the paper its Detail links.

| Id | Area | Question | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

A `const` in `<script module>` is plain module-scope JavaScript, computed once (measured 2026-09-23), so a derivation that belongs to the domain has no reactive home outside an instance script, including in the Resource file the layout assigns to the domain. | [`IDEAS/review-solid-octane.md`](IDEAS/review-solid-octane.md) § Open questions |
| <a id="fjs-d366"></a>FJS-D366 | litestone · toolbelt · sierra | **Is *ordered within a scope, stable under concurrent insert* a column trait (`rank String @rank(scope: stateId)`), or an app pattern with a toolbelt helper?** Driven in linear (Phase 4), 50 concurrent drags into one gap. A fractional key is one write, one broadcast and no conflict per drag. An integer renumbered by the client is 1,625 requests, 1,568 × 409 and 40 shared positions. A server renumber either refuses 49 of 50 under `@version` or writes 2,850 rows and announces 50 ([`FJS-1308`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1308)). Offline, the fractional drag is 1 queued entry and the integer one is 57, of which 55 are refused. What the app still had to do itself: mint the key (the toolbelt has no helper), default it (`@default("a0")` put 46 issues on one key), break ties (a midpoint ties 50 of 50; jitter only helps if EVERY writer jitters), and bound it (≈300 drops at one spot pass 64 characters). | [`IDEAS/manual-order.md`](IDEAS/manual-order.md) § Open questions Q1 |

## Closed

Kept so a stale document cannot reopen them. Newest first.

**Older closures live in [ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md).** This
section holds the recent ones and is trimmed past ~40 rows by `fli register:archive`;
the archive holds the rest, verbatim. An id is never reused, so a `FJS-###` named anywhere in the repo
resolves in one file or the other.

| Id | Title | Closed | How |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="fjs-1715"></a>FJS-1715 | basecamp — **basecamp's requireOutpostSignature is still a per-method hook and has not moved onto createApp({ credentials: [signedRequest(...)] })** | 2026-10-05 | requireOutpostSignature moved onto createApp({ credentials: [outpostCredential(...)] }): keyFor names the machine from X-Service-Method, path and body (OUTPOST_ENDPOINTS replaces OUTPOST_SUBJECT), the machine becomes an outpost:<id> principal carrying its own workspace, and an app-level outpostScope() hook grades it so it reaches only its three endpoints (403 elsewhere, 401 for another machine's id). Proved by api/test/outpost-scope.test.ts (red on the missing export first), the services.test.ts outpost block incl. a signed drain refused, and the full basecamp suite 575/575. · [basecamp core/hooks.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/core/hooks.ts) · [auth/credentials.ts](packages/junction/src/auth/credentials.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1607"></a>FJS-1607 | junction — **The credentials list (FJS-D475) is HTTP-only and bearer is not yet its last entry: the WS upgrade never consults it, verifyApiKey is still reached only inside better-auth's verifySession, and basecamp's requireOutpostSignature has not moved onto signedRequest** | 2026-10-04 | The credentials list was read only by the HTTP path and verifyApiKey only from inside a provider's own verifySession. resolvePrincipal in auth/credentials.ts is now the one loop (declared verifiers, then verifySession, then verifyApiKey on null), called by both _handleRequest and _wsOpen, so a signed upgrade becomes the principal and a refusal closes 4001. test/credentials-ws.test.ts went red on all five behaviors before the change and the junction suite is 2591 green. basecamp's requireOutpostSignature move is split to FJS-1715. · [transport/http.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/http.ts) · [auth/credentials.ts](packages/junction/src/auth/credentials.ts) · [basecamp core/hooks.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/core/hooks.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1714"></a>FJS-1714 | litestone — **removeMany under @@softDelete(cascade) runs its child stamps and @hardDelete deletes outside tx.wrapExclusive, so a cascade refused part way leaves earlier children written** | 2026-10-04 | removeMany's cascade (child stamps and @hardDelete deletes) ran before its tx.wrapExclusive as bare auto-commits, so a refused cascade left earlier children stamped; moved the whole cascade inside the lock with remove()'s nameBlockerForCaller catch. test/foreign-key-error.test.ts (red before, green after; litestone suite 5648 pass). · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1609"></a>FJS-1609 | litestone — **A deleteMany or a soft-delete cascade refused by a Restrict child, and a SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE write, still throw SQLite's bare Error — FJS-1454 covered create, update and delete only** | 2026-10-04 | Cause: asRestrictedDelete was wired into delete only, and asConstraintError had no datatype branch. Fix: deleteMany, hard remove/removeMany and the @hardDelete step of a soft-delete cascade route through refusedDelete/refusedChildDelete + nameBlockerForCaller; SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE becomes a ValidationError on the field path in asConstraintError (all write paths). Proved by test/foreign-key-error.test.ts (5 red before, green after) and the full litestone suite; fli prove's 7 failed drives all carry open: tags · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1613"></a>FJS-1613 | mesa — **A block inside an `<svg>` whose root is `<a>` rendered its SVG children in the HTML namespace on the client** | 2026-10-04 | buildBlock chose svgToFragment from the block's root tags alone, and `<a>` is not an SVG-only tag. The walk now carries the namespace: entering `<svg>` makes it SVG, `<foreignObject>` puts it back to HTML, a portal's children are HTML, and a block that starts in SVG compiles to svgToFragment whatever its root. Pinned by compiler.test.js § a block whose root is <a> inside <svg> renders SVG children (red before; mesa suite 2085 vitest + both browser drives green). The `<g>` workaround is gone from website map.mesa; /map/ under vite dev measured 18 SVG ellipses with a getBBox, and XHTML with the fix disabled · [compiler.js](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1627"></a>FJS-1627 | mesa — **On <mesa:window>, <mesa:document> and <mesa:body>, the capture, once, passive, debounce and throttle modifiers are dropped without a word — <mesa:window on:keydown\|capture={f}> registers a bubble-phase listener — while the same modifiers on an element are honoured and an unknown one is refused** | 2026-10-04 | Cause: the global-target branch of buildBlock kept only the four guard modifiers and emitted addGlobalEvent with no options, while the element branch built listenerOpts and refused unknown modifiers. Fix: one applyEventModifiers in compiler.js serves both, and the global call passes the options (addGlobalEvent already took them). Proof: test/global-event-modifiers.test.js (five red before, six green after); mesa vitest 2084 and both gating browser drives pass. example verify fails on planDetail.newWindowEmpty and a litestone client-closed warning, outside this diff (FJS-1708). · [compiler.js:4911](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) · [compiler.js:6835](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) · [compiler.js](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) · [mesa AGENTS.md](packages/mesa/AGENTS.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1628"></a>FJS-1628 | sierra — **test/resource-schema-modes.test.js § a column is marked only where something declared it fails (11 columns marked, 1 expected: Flow.ownerId…) and fs-allow-plugin.test.js imports bun:test under vitest** | 2026-10-04 | Did not reproduce on 2026-10-04: both files pass under vitest (fs-allow-plugin.test.js imports from 'vitest'; resource-schema-modes.test.js 'a column is marked only where something declared it' passes) and the whole sierra suite is green, 119 files / 1875 tests. Neither file differs from HEAD, so the failure was the shared tree mid-edit while FJS-1244's litestone work was in flight, not a defect in either test. · [resource-schema-modes.test.js](packages/sierra/test/resource-schema-modes.test.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1713"></a>FJS-1713 | litestone — **updateMany never grades a post-update rule, so a bulk write moves a row anywhere update() refuses — tenancy's own generated deny included: updateMany({ data: { workspaceId: 20 } }) moves a workspace-10 caller's row into workspace 20.** | 2026-10-04 | updateMany put only the update rule (the row before) into its WHERE and never ran checkPostUpdatePolicy, so a bulk write moved rows past tenancy's generated post-update deny and any hand-written one. Where the model has a post-update rule, updateMany now takes RETURNING * and grades every changed row inside the unit, so one refusal rolls the batch back; verifyTenantIsolation's post-update crossing tries updateMany after update and reports the leak with the grading stubbed out. test/tenancy.test.ts § a row cannot be moved out of its tenant (two cases, red before); litestone suite 5642/0; example verify:tenants green. · [FJS-1712](#fjs-1712) · [test/tenant-isolation.test.ts](packages/litestone/test/tenant-isolation.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1712"></a>FJS-1712 | litestone — **A create rule that read a parent's column was evaluated against the real parent row, whatever the caller may read, so a caller who chose the foreign key and a payload value read a hidden or another tenant's parent one guess at a time.** | 2026-10-04 | hiddenParents now runs before the create policy and every refusal carries the set of relations whose named parent the caller cannot read (all of them, not the first); checkCreatePolicy takes that set and evalPath answers a hop across one as null, as across a missing parent. check() answers false there too, but only where it would have looked the row up: with no target row policy it admits without a lookup, missing parent included, and refusing the hidden one alone was a new oracle (a parent hidden by its @@gate). create, createMany and upsertMany. Not reached by update: a move onto a hidden parent is thrown before the post-update rule runs, and an unchanged one is read for real so it stays editable. A field-level write rule reading a parent was probed and is unobservable — the row is refused before anything reads it. Proved by test/hidden-parent.test.ts § a create rule reading a parent the caller cannot read, 5 cases, each guard red under its own mutation; litestone 5642, basecamp 570, junction 2584, orion 598. · [FJS-1704](#fjs-1704) · [`FJS-D576`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d576) · [test/hidden-parent.test.ts](packages/litestone/test/hidden-parent.test.ts) |

Pinned by tables.spec.js § table-wrap: a hidden header label scrolls with the table, not the page (offsetParent was the body before; css suite 514 passing) · [tables.css](packages/css/src/components/tables.css) |

Pinned by test/seal-immutable.test.ts (red before, litestone suite 5635 pass). · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) |

The growth is spread over the shared tree's in-flight mesa, sierra and schema edits, none of which is large enough to cut; paid with FJS_OFFLINE_BASELINE=update (FJS-D302), baseline now 941. | <a id="fjs-1654"></a>FJS-1654 | litestone — **A where naming a @from field through a relation (where: { parent: { is: { kidCount: { gt: 1 } } } }) fails with SQLite 'no such column: t.kidCount' rather than working or being refused by name, even as system** | 2026-10-04 | The relation hop (relationFilterOn in client.js) built the target's where with no @from map, so a @from/@derived field read as a column of the aliased target. It now passes the target's subqueries via a new subquerySqlFor(alias) on the fromMap entry, so they correlate on the hop's own alias at every depth. Proved by two cases added to test/from-policy.test.ts (system, unpolicied caller, nested depth); litestone suite 5633 pass. · [test/from-policy.test.ts](packages/litestone/test/from-policy.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1668"></a>FJS-1668 | junction — **The Resend mailer's fetch still has no timeout, retry or breaker, and no fli check rule sees a literal apiKey in a battery constructor** | 2026-10-04 | Cause: createResendMailer's fetch carried no signal, and no fli check rule graded the raw apiKey option FJS-D219 kept. Fix: both Resend endpoints now fetch under AbortSignal.timeout(30s) and a timeout throws 'Resend API timed out after 30000ms'; retry and breaker are left out on purpose — a send is not idempotent, so a retry after a lost response is a second email, and an app that needs either routes through Conduit, which owns both. New warn rule battery-raw-secret reads api/ source for createResendMailer({ apiKey }) (literal, env read or shorthand) and names credentials + apiKeyRef. Proved by test/resend-credentials.test.ts and the cli checks.test.js (red before, green after), junction test (2584 pass), typecheck clean; fli prove's example verify, basecamp verify and verify:live failures are not mail-bound (verify:live open FJS-1382/FJS-1371). · [mail/index.ts](packages/junction/src/mail/index.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1680"></a>FJS-1680 | conduit — **A broker target's policy numbers and trace headers are not applied to its dial, though FJS-D235 says they apply unchanged.** | 2026-10-04 | BrokerTransport dialed with auth only. It now takes the router's timeout_ms (dial bound, closes a silent upgrade into the reconnect backoff) and max_response_bytes (frame cap: dropped, unacked), and conduit.subscribe hands it the trace headers for a trace:true target, asked per dial. Proved by three new conduit.test.ts broker tests, red before; example verify:stripe/notify/pay pass. · [broker.ts](packages/conduit/src/transports/broker.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1684"></a>FJS-1684 | sierra · basecamp — **Leaving one `[id]` screen for another re-runs the old screen's `$: page.params.id` watch with the NEW route's id while it is still mounted.** `apps/[id]` → `deployments/[id]` called `apps.get` with the deployment's id and logged a 404 on every navigation. This is `FJS-1018`'s unconfirmed router hypothesis (`page.params` assigned before `page.route`) observed directly, with the new id rather than `undefined`; a screen whose load writes would act on the wrong record. | 2026-10-04 | Cause was in sierra's ChainRenderer, not the router's commit order: Component came from an entry memo one derived layer below the remount key that also reads entry, so on an [id] to [id] or [id] to list navigation the key block re-ran first and mounted the screen being left (stale Component) with the new params. Component and the key now both read chain[depth]. Proved by packages/sierra/test/screen-leave-router.test.js (real router + RouterView + ChainRenderer; red at HEAD, green with the fix) and the sierra suite (1871 pass). · [FJS-1018](#fjs-1018) · [verify-screens.mjs](packages/basecamp/web/test/verify-screens.mjs) |

| <a id="fjs-1706"></a>FJS-1706 | basecamp — **verify-screens: DELETE /servers/<offline server> answers 422 where the drive expects a removal, so 'the widget's server is removed' fails** | 2026-10-04 | Cause: servers.remove soft-deleted the row, then wrote the 'removed' ServerEvent naming it; since FJS-D576 a foreign key is a read, so the event was refused as a parent the caller cannot see (ForeignKeyError, 422) after the removal had happened. | <a id="fjs-1707"></a>FJS-1707 | conduit — **withCache sent one inner call per concurrent miss, so a burst against a cold cache made one vault request per send()** | 2026-10-04 | Cause: withCache cached only after inner.get resolved and tracked nothing in flight. Proved by two new conduit.test.ts tests (burst of 100 was red at 100 inner calls) and verify:notify/stripe/pay green. · [credentials.ts](packages/conduit/src/credentials.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1018"></a>FJS-1018 | basecamp — **`Portal service 'null' not found` still has no screen behind it — but the two conflations that made it unreadable are gone, so what is left is one 404 in a log rather than a fault report.** The original row carried three claims and two of them are now closed. **One sentence answered three questions**: no id at all (`$.id` is null), a caller that interpolated an empty value (the STRING `'null'`, which `!id` does not catch), and an appliance genuinely absent from the registry. `portal.get` and `portal.create` now separate them by STATUS — a value that cannot be an id is a 400 saying so, a real id nobody serves stays a 404 — and `ServiceHealthBody`'s guard was widened to the string forms, since a template writes those from a variable that was not set. **And the app logged every refusal as a fault**: the `error:` hook in `api/src/app.ts` wrote EVERY thrown service error at ERROR, so an ordinary miss, a stranger's 401 and every 403 the gate exists to give all read as breakage and buried the 5xx that was. It now grades on the status junction already decided (`ctx.error` is a FrameworkError and `code` is its status — read, not re-derived), two tiers: 5xx is a fault, 4xx is the app working. **What is NOT settled is the caller.** A router hypothesis — `_navigate` assigns `page.params` before `page.route`, so a detail page watching `page.params.id` would re-read with `undefined` while it is still mounted — was probed in sierra's own harness and the probe was INCONCLUSIVE: the control showed no page field notified the effect at all, so the instrument was dead and the reading says nothing either way. Reproduce as the original row said, by capturing the WS frame — the id is on `meta.id` — with the 400 above now telling a broken call from a missing appliance in the log itself | 2026-10-04 | Caller found: the appliance detail page. Leaving /portal/:id/ by its back link re-runs the page's `$: page.params.id` watch while it is still mounted, with no id, so load() called portal.get(undefined) — the 400 added earlier logged exactly that. load() now returns when the route has no id. Proved by a new verify-screens.mjs drive (enter /portal/edge/, leave by the link, read the API log): red before, green after. The general fault across the other [id] screens is FJS-1684. · [portal.service.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/services/portal/portal.service.ts) · [app.ts error hook](packages/basecamp/api/src/app.ts) · [ServiceHealthBody.mesa](packages/basecamp/web/src/components/widgets/ServiceHealthBody.mesa) · [services.test.ts](packages/basecamp/api/test/services.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1458"></a>FJS-1458 | auth — **Under `tenancy { strategy row }`, `POST /auth/register` makes a person who can reach nothing, and auth offers no step at which the app could give them a tenant. `onRegister` runs before the row exists and may only refuse; `createUser` writes `email`, `name` and `role` and nothing else, so the tenant column cannot be passed through it** | 2026-10-04 | Cause: createUser wrote user + credential with no step after, and onRegister runs before the row, so a row-tenanted app had nowhere to write the tenant column. Fix (FJS-D520): onRegistered({ user, db }) in the new registerUser() helper, which wraps the row, its credential and the hook in one sys.$transaction for createUser and the OAuth create branch; a throw rolls it all back and the session is built from the row re-read after the hook. Proved by flows.test.ts +2 and oauth-resolve.test.ts +1 (the first two red with the option ignored); auth suite 417 pass, example verify:users and verify:oauth pass. · [auth.ts `createUser`](packages/auth/auth.ts) · [plugin.ts `/register`](packages/auth/plugin.ts) |

| <a id="fjs-1014"></a>FJS-1014 | conduit — **[`FJS-D235`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d235) is in force and names three things the tree does not have, so a ruling in the register points at nothing in the code.** Probed 2026-09-07: `TargetKind` is `provider`, `outpost`, `local` and carries no `broker` ([types.ts:12](packages/conduit/src/types.ts#L12)); `Protocol` carries `nats` marked *defined, not implemented in V1* ([types.ts:10](packages/conduit/src/types.ts#L10)) and the router answers that case with `NotImplementedTransport` ([router.ts:129](packages/conduit/src/router.ts#L129)); and conduit calls `app.registerHealthCheck` **nowhere**. So the ruling is decided and unbuilt, which is the shape [`FJS-D190`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d190) named one tier up — a settled question with no artefact reads exactly like a built one from every document that cites it, and D235 is now cited by [`IDEAS/inbound-integrations.md`](IDEAS/inbound-integrations.md). **What the row exists to protect is the obligation clause, which shapes the feature rather than decorating it**: a subscription that has silently stopped consuming looks exactly like a quiet broker, so a broker target reports when it last received and whether its connection is up, through `app.registerHealthCheck`, **or it does not ship**. That is the same failure this repo has been bitten by twice one realm over — a scrape that died and a value inside its threshold draw the same flat line — and it is the half most likely to be dropped by whoever builds the descriptor first, because a `kind` in a switch is a morning's work and a liveness reading is not. **Three pieces, in the order they are decidable**: the `broker` kind on `TargetKind` with the seven policy numbers, the credential reference, the encoding and the trace headers applying unchanged; the dial-then-receive loop, which is `transports/websocket.ts` with a different envelope; and the seam sentence — a received message is dispatched under its OWN id and the ack follows the dispatch, never before it, since an ack before the handoff is a lost order and an ack after an idempotent dispatch is a redelivery that costs nothing. **Capped for free by [`FJS-D153`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d153)**: a connector to a named vendor is not in this repo, so what is owed is a descriptor and a seam, never a Kafka client. The obligation clause ships with it: each subscription registers conduit:<target> through registerHealth (the plugin points it at app.registerHealthCheck; reads connection up) and stats().subscriptions reports connected, last_received_at, received. | <a id="fjs-949"></a>FJS-949 | junction — **A stopped app cannot be started again, and until now it said so by dying on the router.** `stop()` clears `started` but the router is built once and refuses middleware afterwards, so start → stop → start reached `security-headers` and threw `Cannot add middleware after the router is built` — an internal sentence about a router for a caller who asked to start an app. [`FJS-950`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-950) made the refusal say what is actually true; this row is the capability behind it. **Measured while writing the control for that refusal**, which had assumed restart worked: nothing in junction’s 2274 tests restarts an app, so the shape had never been exercised.
