| <a id="fjs-401"></a>FJS-401 | junction · litestone — **A declared input gets a `type`'s validators and not its transforms, and wiring them naively would make the two boundaries disagree about what is VALID.** `methods: [{ method, input: 'PayOrder' }]` validates a custom method's payload against a `type T { … }` in the seed — required keys, types, `@length`, `@gte`, the author's `x-messages`, all of it. What it does not get is `@trim`/`@lower`/`@upper`/`@slug`: those are enforced in litestone's `applyTransforms` at the Data boundary and are emitted into JSON Schema **nowhere**, so nothing downstream can see them, and a custom method's payload never becomes a model write and therefore never meets them. A `type` whose whole point is a normalized value is silently un-normalized. The reason this is a filed question rather than a small patch is ORDER: litestone transforms BEFORE validating (`client.js:5172`, `applyTransforms` and then `validate`), junction's `FieldDef.transform` runs AFTER (`schema.ts:241`), so `@trim @length(3,12)` on `'  ab  '` fails at the Data boundary and passes through a service — the two would not merely produce different output, they would disagree about what is legal, which is the shape Invariant 4 exists to prevent. Doing it properly is three moves and each has a decision in it: litestone emits the transform names (a new keyword in a document with a committed snapshot and three readers); junction gains a PRE-validation slot rather than reusing `transform`, so nothing existing moves; and the implementations want one owner, which by the shape of `/inflect` and `/directives` is a `@frontierjs/toolbelt` subpath both packages read — `@slug` in particular is not a one-liner anyone should write twice. Sierra would then get client-side transforms for free, which it also does not have today. Measured 2026-08-22 and asserted still-broken in `packages/junction/test/method-input.test.ts` — a transform that starts crossing turns that test red rather than leaving it stale. **Amended 2026-09-28, *Portal, the search stressor* (bangs): it is not only a custom method's input — an ordinary MODEL write through a service meets the same disagreement, at two boundaries before litestone.** `Bang.trigger String @lower @regex("^[a-z0-9]{1,12}$")`. Litestone alone does what `fli db:explain @lower` says (*lowercase before validation*): `SYS1` as system and `USR1` as a user both stored as `sys1`/`usr1`, measured. But `POST /api/bangs { trigger: 'UP1' }` answers **400 `trigger: trigger format is invalid`** from junction's `validateField` (`schema.ts`), whose `def.lowercase` exists and is set by nothing because the JSON Schema carries no transform. And the generated `<Form>` refuses first, in Chrome: *trigger is not in the expected format* under the box, `aria-invalid`, no request sent (sierra `field-rules.js`, the `pattern` rule). So the row's premise that a model write *meets* the transforms holds only for a caller that bypasses the service: a person typing `W` in the generated form for a `@lower` trigger cannot save it, and the error blames the format. Any `@trim`/`@lower`/`@upper` beside `@regex`, `@length` or `@startsWith` is exposed; `@trim @length` on `' ab '` is the row's own example, now reachable from a form. Pinned in portal `api/test/bangs.test.ts` (*FJS-401 pin*: the service's 400 beside litestone's lowering, green while this is open); D482's decision now has a model-write caller and a form | 2026-10-04 | Cause: litestone applied @trim/@lower/@upper/@slug before validating but emitted them into JSON Schema nowhere, so junction (service payloads, declared method inputs, model writes) and sierra (generated forms) graded the raw value and disagreed about what is valid. Fix per FJS-D482 A: one owner, @frontierjs/toolbelt/transforms (transform/isTransform/TRANSFORM_NAMES), which litestone's transformValue now calls; litestone emits ordered x-transforms from applyValidators (columns and type fields); junction reads it into FieldDef.transforms and runs it ahead of the checks in validateField (FieldDef.transform unchanged, still post-validation); sierra carries x-transforms and applies it at the top of _checkConstraints. Proof: toolbelt transforms.spec.js, litestone messages.test.ts, junction method-input.test.ts (the old still-broken pin flipped to assert trim reaches the method and the trimmed length is what is graded; red with the read stubbed), sierra field-messages.test.js (red with the call stubbed); suites green in toolbelt, litestone, junction (plus typecheck clean) and sierra; example verify:widget green. · [client.js:5172](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js#L5172) · [schema.ts:241](packages/junction/src/core/schema.ts#L241) · [jsonschema.js](packages/litestone/src/jsonschema.js) |
| <a id="fjs-659"></a>FJS-659 | junction · conduit — **Junction's mail battery re-hand-rolls the ring conduit exists to own, and it ships a second credential convention while doing it.** `createResendMailer` (`src/mail/index.ts`) holds a raw `apiKey` in a closure, hardcodes `https://api.resend.com`, and calls `fetch` with **no retry, no timeout, no deadline, no breaker, no observers and no line in `/metrics`**. Dialing out directly is CORRECT and is not the defect — `IMail` is a contract with a working default, which is what keeps `@frontierjs/conduit` optional, and the escalation path already exists in both directions (`packages/basecamp/api/src/core/mailer.ts` is `IMail` over `app.conduit`; junction's email CAMPAIGN tier refuses to run without conduit and says so by name). **What is wrong is the second convention**: conduit's rule is that a target carries a credential *reference* and material is resolved at send time so it stays out of the registry, out of hooks and off the management routes, and this adapter takes the secret itself as a constructor argument — so one framework teaches two incompatible habits about the most sensitive value an integration holds. The narrow fix is a shared resilient `fetch` helper plus a ref-shaped credential option; the wide one is a conduit-backed default when `app.conduit` is present. **It was two adapters and is now one, which is the precedent rather than a shrunken scope**: the AI half named `createAnthropicModel` and an OpenAI-shaped adapter, and both were DELETED by [FJS-903](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-903) / [FJS-D215](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d215) rather than fixed, on the ground that a connector to a named vendor does not live in a boundary package ([FJS-D153](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d153)). Resend is the same shape and the same question, and the reason it is not the same answer has to be stated here or the two rows disagree: `IMail` is a declared CONTRACT with implementations an app selects between, where `IAIModel` had one vendor each | 2026-10-04 | Cause: createResendMailer took the secret as a constructor argument, a second credential convention beside Conduit's reference-resolved one. Fix per FJS-D219: a structural CredentialSource ({ get(ref) }) plus apiKeyRef, resolved on every send, so a Conduit CredentialResolver passes in unchanged and an app without Conduit passes { get: ref => process.env[ref] }; an unresolved ref throws by name before any fetch; the literal apiKey stays, as a union so both together is a type error. Docs and the notifications example teach the ref form. Proved by test/resend-credentials.test.ts (red before, green after), junction test (2561 pass) and typecheck clean; the drives are not mail-bound and fli prove's failures (example verify, basecamp verify needing --reset, verify:live open FJS-1382/FJS-1371) read as shared-port/DB state. · [mail/index.ts:153](packages/junction/src/mail/index.ts#L153) · [credentials.ts](packages/conduit/src/credentials.ts) · [FJS-D177](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d177) · [FJS-D215](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d215) · [FJS-D219](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d219) |

A signal already aborted is refused before admission. `http`, `unix`, `websocket` and the stub honor it. **The per-send `deadline_ms` the row asked for was not built**: `AbortSignal.timeout(ms)` is that deadline, and a second spelling expiring as `timeout` would open a healthy target's breaker, which is `FJS-1409` again. | <a id="fjs-d566"></a>FJS-D566 | litestone — **How does a row policy say *the caller is one of this row's members* — a to-many test `rel.any(…)` compiled as a correlated EXISTS, or a `claim … from` that yields a list read with `in`?** | 2026-09-30 | Ruled [`FJS-D566`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d566): **A** — a to-many test in the policy language: `<hasMany>.any(<expr>)`, where `<expr>` names only the CHILD's columns and `auth()`. The SQL half compiles it to a correlated `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM child WHERE child.fk = parent.pk AND …)`. The JS half runs the same `SELECT 1 … LIMIT 1` through `ctx.readDb`, as `evalPath` does. `FJS-D221`'s bound holds: one hop, so `interview.scorecards.any(…)`, a path inside the parentheses, and a nested `.any` are each refused at parse, and the first refusal now names the to-many test rather than *crosses two relations*. On `create` no child exists yet, so it answers false (fails closed), which is the answer a null-FK path already gives. `x-gate` answers `unknown`, per D221. The five sites read `members.any(userId == auth().id)`, `memberships.any(workspaceId == auth().workspaceId)`, `memberships.any(userId == auth().id)`, `grants.any(granteeId == auth().id && status == 'active')` and `scorecards.any(interviewerId == auth().id && submittedAt != null)`, and every copy and the `teamIds` resolver are deleted. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1413"></a>FJS-1413 | conduit · junction — **Conduit's default trace hands every target the inbound request's correlation id (`X-Request-Id`, and the same id as `traceparent`'s trace id). Junction writes that id on every audit row of the request, so a rented provider's log of a query can be joined to the person who typed it.** The default exists on purpose (`FJS-742`: a target's logs could not be joined to the request that caused them), and between two services one team owns that is right. Sent to a stranger that holds the query, it is the join key. **Measured** 2026-09-27 in *Portal, the search stressor*, through the real API. The fake provider received `q=portal-canary-7f3e-http` with `x-request-id: 9d818898-…` and `traceparent: 00-9d818898…-…-01`. Our response carried the same id, and so did our audit row for that request, `user update accountId` (the account a first search makes), beside the user's id and IP. So a provider can learn who searched what on any request that writes an audited row. In Portal that is the first search. In an app whose search stamps anything `@@log`'d (a `lastSearchedAt`) it is every search. The request log would carry the id on every search too, and does not only because of `FJS-1414`. A client's own `X-Request-Id` is forwarded verbatim. The switch is conduit-wide: `trace: () => null` sends neither header, to any target (pinned). The hook receives the request, so it could decide per target, but the plugin's default (`junctionTrace`) is not exported. Keeping traces to one's own services while dropping them to providers therefore means rewriting it. Candidate: `trace: false` on a target descriptor, a target declaring that it is a third party. `compliance-from-the-seed.md` § 5's outbound surface report would want to read the same fact | 2026-09-30 | The junction plugin's default trace sent the inbound correlation id (as X-Request-Id and as traceparent's trace id) to every conduit target, and junction writes that id on every audit row of the request, so a provider holding a query held the join to who typed it. Per FJS-D507 a target now gets the trace only when its descriptor states `trace: true`: `withTrace` asks the transport's `traced`, and `trace` joins `EXTRA_KEYS`. Proved by junction-integration.test.ts (an undeclared target receives neither header, red with the gate stubbed out), the trace-context suite now declaring its targets, portal's never-keep.test.ts pin flipped, and verify:stripe/notify/pay. · [portal api/test/never-keep.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/test/never-keep.test.ts) · [portal fakes/canary.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/fakes/canary.ts) · [plugin.ts `junctionTrace`](packages/conduit/src/plugin.ts) · [compliance-from-the-seed.md § 5](IDEAS/compliance-from-the-seed.md) |

| <a id="fjs-371"></a>FJS-371 | junction — **An inbound credential that is not a bearer token has no seam, so signature auth is 90 lines of app code.** Junction resolves every `Authorization: Bearer` through `IAuth.verifySession` and calls `verifyApiKey` nowhere itself — only the better-auth provider reaches it, from inside its own `verifySession` ([better-auth.ts:87](packages/junction/src/auth/providers/better-auth.ts#L87)) — one door, documented as a live hazard — and a machine caller proving itself with an HMAC over the request has no way in except a hook the app registers app-level and must remember to keep ahead of every new machine-facing method. basecamp's `requireOutpostSignature` is that hook: canonical string, replay window, fail-closed in four directions, registered app-level *precisely so a new method cannot inherit the sessionScope exemption without it* ([FJS-349](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-349) is what happens when it is missing — an unsigned POST moved a server online and pointed the fleet at an attacker's host). | <a id="fjs-598"></a>FJS-598 | repo · litestone — **The tool built to find holes in the checks runs in CI only over a tutorial's schema — neither app's is ever mutated.** Corrected 2026-09-13: the `tutor` phase (full tier only) ends `tutor:test` with `litestone mutate` over the lesson's own schema (`commands/tutor/_steps-test/05-mutate.md`), so the tool is proven to RUN and nothing grades `example` or `basecamp` with it. `litestone mutate` mutates the SCHEMA and runs the checks derived from the ORIGINAL against a database built from the mutant, so a surviving mutant is a statement about the SUITE rather than about the schema — and every survivor it has reported so far named a check that did not exist yet and now does (`verifyGateLadder`, `verifyConstraints`, `verifyFieldProtection` and `verifyRowPolicies` are each in the default set because a mutant survived without one). Nothing re-asks the question after a schema changes, so a `@@gate` added to a model no fixture reaches is ungraded and silent, which is the same shape as a snapshot that stops being checked. **What has to be decided before a phase exists is the tier, and it is measured**: `example` scores **100% — 159/159 graded in 239s**, 15 of them refused by the parser or the loader and the other 144 killed by a verdict disagreement. `basecamp` is 328 mutants, runnable since [FJS-597](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-597) and needing `ENCRYPTION_KEY` in the environment — and it does NOT scale from example's rate: a full run was still going at **25 minutes** when it was killed. The per-mutant cost is a property of the SCHEMA rather than of the tool, and basecamp's is the expensive shape — 45 models, 34 of them carrying row policies, under declared row tenancy, so `verifyRowPolicies` and `verifyTenantIsolation` are grading a far larger surface per mutant than example's 26 models ask for. So the pair is well past half an hour and a pre-push tier costing ~40s is out by two orders of magnitude. Full tier, a nightly, or a `--kinds` subset on the fast one. **The 100% is the argument FOR the phase rather than against it**: the four checks already see everything this schema can express, so a phase added today starts green and fires the first time somebody declares something they cannot see — which is precisely the ratchet, and precisely what nobody notices without one. So a score BASELINE is the other half, the way `scripts/typecheck-baselines.json` is (Invariant 14): a phase that fails on any survivor fails on the first schema anybody adds, and a phase that prints a percentage nobody reads is the `access` phase's problem without the `access` phase's excuse. | 2026-09-30 | CI now has a full-tier `mutate` phase (scripts/ci.mjs) running `litestone mutate --json` over example and basecamp and failing a score below each app's floor in scripts/mutate-baselines.json, which ratchets up (FJS-D476). Both run under a five-kind subset (deny/encrypted/guarded-drop, relator-loosen/tighten): measured, a mutant costs ~30s and example is ~490 mutants. Floors are measured, not assumed: example 17/17 = 100 in 5m, basecamp 14/22 = 64 in ~11m; its survivors are FJS-1594 and the new deny-drop row. · [ci.mjs](scripts/ci.mjs) · [mutate.js](packages/litestone/src/mutate.js) · [test/mutate.md](packages/cli/commands/test/mutate.md) |

| <a id="fjs-1531"></a>FJS-1531 | example — **The browser drives leaked their Chrome profiles into `/tmp`, ~500MB a run, until the disk was full** | 2026-09-28 | 33GB across ~16,000 `fjs-*` directories in three weeks, found with `/` at 100%. Each drive made its profile with `mkdtempSync` and removed it after `chrome.kill()`, which FJS-361 had already measured not to work: the removal succeeds and Chrome's children write the profile back, so a passing run leaked too; a throw or a signal skipped the removal. `example/web/test/lib/chrome-profile.mjs` now owns the profile for all 20 drives in `web/`, `site/` and `extension/`: at exit or on SIGINT/TERM/HUP it `pkill`s every process whose command line names the profile, waits 300ms, removes it; `tempDir()` reaps a previous run's past an hour. Probed with real Chrome under node and bun: a clean exit, a throw and a SIGTERM each left no profile and no process, and a SIGKILLed run's profile went on the next run. The drives in basecamp, litestone, sierra and `website/` keep their own teardown and were not changed. |

| <a id="fjs-711"></a>FJS-711 | caravan — **Caravan is correct under concurrency and unbounded everywhere a value is not a row: the cron grammar, the retry ladder, the payload and the missing operator verbs.** Umbrella for the remaining caravan findings. **`caravan-9`** — split out and **closed as [`FJS-767`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-767)**: the grammar is `@frontierjs/toolbelt/cron` now, one owner for two parsers, and everything above is refused at registration naming the field and the bound. The suggested remedy — *a boot warning where `nextRuns()` is null* — is superseded rather than done: with the sets bounded and an impossible day of month refused at parse, there is no expression left that registers and cannot fire. **`caravan-11`** — split out and **closed as [`FJS-1384`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1384)**: the ladder is jittered upward now. As found: the retry ladder is fixed, un-jittered and three attempts, plateaus at the last delay, and `retry()` resets `attempts = 0` — three jobs failing at one instant retried at gaps of `[60000, 60000, 60000]`, so 500 jobs against a downed provider all return at T+60 s exactly, which is the thundering herd a queue is bought to prevent; conduit has `backoffWithJitter` one package over. **`caravan-12`** — ruled [`FJS-D198`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d198): **Queue** is the noun, the set stays derived from `queueConf`, and the missing verbs are OPERATOR verbs — gated at ADMINISTRATOR or above, audited, and in no service file — where `dispatch`/`handle`/`schedule` are app verbs. A paused queue must be distinguishable from an idle one in `stats()`, and `queue('typo')` must refuse by name rather than `ensureQueue` creating it. Present and good are priority, delay, per-queue concurrency, dedup, cancel-pending, manual retry, cron and retention; absent are a per-queue RATE LIMIT (the commonest reason a queue exists, with no correct workaround per replica), cancelling a RUNNING job (`timeout` is advisory and `ctx` carries no `AbortSignal`), purge (**pause, resume and drain were built 2026-09-12** — `app.jobs.queue(name)`, a `queue_pauses` row the claim statement itself reads, ADMINISTRATOR over HTTP, `pausedMs` in `stats()`, `queue('typo')` refusing by name, and `fli check`'s `queue-operator-verb`; an incident can stop a queue without killing the process now, and purge is the verb of the four still missing), bulk dispatch (1000 jobs is 1000 fsyncs), and progress reporting. **`caravan-13`** — ruled [`FJS-D480`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d480), split out and **closed as [`FJS-1514`](#fjs-1514)**: dispatch refuses what JSON would not hand back, by job and key path, and a payload over 256 KB; the ruling's type half (`defineJob<T>` requiring a JSON-plain `T`) is not built, because a recursive JSON type refuses every `interface` payload. **`caravan-14`**: a job whose recorded actor has been deleted burns the whole retry ladder on a permanent condition and dies with an error about a principal rather than about the work, with the handler running zero times — a staff member leaving, a reseeded fixture or a deleted tenant, where the receipt is still owed; ruled [`FJS-D202`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d202): `onMissingActor` declared beside `maxAttempts`, **defaulting to `'fail'`** — fast and terminal, no ladder, the error naming the job and the actor id — because `'system'` as a default completes a departed staff member's work at level 8, above every gate the person passed. `'system'` stays available per job. **Split out and closed as [`FJS-1383`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1383)**: `onMissingActor` built 2026-09-26. Cites `FJS-D193`: a delivery needs a nameable principal. Promote any of these to its own id when it is worked | 2026-09-29 | An umbrella, not one defect: caravan-9, -11, -13 and -14 were each split out and closed (FJS-767, FJS-1384, FJS-1514, FJS-1383), and pause/resume/drain were built under FJS-D198. | <a id="fjs-706"></a>FJS-706 | junction — **The service pipeline's construction-time surfaces return an object where they should return a verdict, so ten authoring mistakes compile green.** Umbrella for the medium and low core findings. **What is left, 2026-09-13, is three**: `core-8`, `core-12`, and `core-13`'s `health` shadow — every other sub-finding below is closed or ruled. Each is `D1`'s shape — fail-closed on the caller, fail-open on the author. **Ruled [`FJS-D199`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d199)**: an unknown AUTHORING key is refused by name, with every one of them reported at `start()` rather than thrown one boot at a time; what a CALLER sends stays tolerant, and there is no permitting flag. **`core-4`** — promoted to [`FJS-942`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-942) and closed. **`core-5`** — the two silences are [`FJS-945`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-945) and closed; the `around` half is [`FJS-946`](#fjs-946), which is a change to the shipped hook contract rather than a silence. **`core-6`** — promoted to [`FJS-939`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-939) and closed for the SECTION half; the nested half is [`FJS-940`](#fjs-940). **`core-7`** — the two lifecycle defects are [`FJS-950`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-950) and closed, and restart is [`FJS-949`](#fjs-949); the `ready()` half is not a defect — a throw in `ready` being logged while already serving is what this package's `CLAUDE.md` documents and `boot()` is the phase for anything that must succeed. **`core-8`**: measured, 20 concurrent creates with a 50 ms after-hook cost 60 ms with `transactional: false` and **1023 ms** with it on, fully serialized on SQLite's write lock — wants a bounded `transactional: { timeout }`. **`core-9`** — the one defect in it is [`FJS-951`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-951) and closed; the other three claims are measured DELIBERATE, with the rationale already written into the code, and the sub-finding's framing was wrong about them. **`core-10`** — **closed 2026-09-04, and the filing overstated it.** The guard IS structural and a row carrying `kind`/`object`/`data` did match; the `kind: 'list'` variant is the damaging one, because a list envelope is kept whole, so `protect()` stripped inside the row's own `data` column and the row's protected siblings went out intact. **The trigger was then measured rather than argued**: across every `.lite` here — this repo's apps, the seven imported real-world corpora and the 188-model `openmrp` fixture — no model carries even TWO of the three columns together, `object` occurs once in total, and `kind` is an enum nearly everywhere, so it holds `percent` and never `'single'`. **The branch it exploits also has no producer**: nothing outside `core/envelope.ts` imports `single()`/`list()`, and every legitimate pre-wrapped path (cache hit, idempotency replay, a `before` hook answering the call) sets `ctx.result` and skips the method, so it never reaches `raw` — disabling the passthrough left the suite at 1985 pass, 0 fail. So no Symbol brand: `errors` is a fourth discriminant, one line, and the brand is the answer if a real schema ever hits it. **Deleting the passthrough was the other candidate and is worse** — an app that did return an envelope from a custom method would have it double-wrapped, nested inside `data`, in silence. The tripwire carries the exact colliding row in both `kind` variants, with the same row plus `errors` beside it as the control. **`core-11`** — the ambient half is ruled `FJS-D198`'s neighbour [`FJS-D200`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d200): `$` is a CLOSED set the framework owns, so a timeout and an `AbortSignal` may land there and app state may not. No per-call timeout, no `AbortSignal` into a call, no trace parent (a nested call is a sibling), and `app.service('x')` is `Promise<unknown>` on the server while `ServiceTypes` types the browser. **`core-12`**: `registerErrorMapper` and `setServiceCache` are process-global across apps, `app.claim()` is bypassable by plain assignment, and `ready()` runs after `listen`. **`core-13`** — the loader's three silences are [`FJS-944`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-944) and closed; the `health` shadow is a ROUTING question with a different owner and stays here, unworked. Promote any of these to its own id when it is worked | 2026-09-29 | Umbrella split out, nothing left under it. core-8 is ruled FJS-D471: no bounded-wait option, and slow after-hook work goes through ctx.enqueue. core-12 is promoted to FJS-1510 and the health shadow (core-13) to FJS-1511, as the row asked once they were worked. Every other sub-finding was already closed or ruled under its own id. · [service.ts](packages/junction/src/core/service.ts) · [app.ts](packages/junction/src/core/app.ts) · [loader.ts](packages/junction/src/core/loader.ts) · [config](packages/junction/src/config/index.ts) · [envelope.ts](packages/junction/src/core/envelope.ts) · [audit § 3.1](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/afba85f4-efe9-4959-92ca-a1b010dd2a16) |

| <a id="fjs-1493"></a>FJS-1493 | mesa · sierra — **`layout:` in a `.md` file's frontmatter was parsed into `ctx.layout` and read by nothing, so a Markdown file could not name the component that wraps it — which is how every ksite block is written.** | 2026-09-28 | Found by the *ksite stressor*, Phase 1. **Mesa wraps and Sierra names.** `compileMd` takes `layouts` (name → file) and wraps the body in the named component. The layout receives every frontmatter key as a prop, which the parent may override, plus the parent's `class` and `$attributes`, and it gets the body both as element children and as the `children` prop. A name the map does not hold is a compile error listing the names it does, and `layout: false` or no map wraps nothing. The wrap happens after Markdown because CommonMark cannot carry a tag line with `{...$attributes}` on it. Sierra's `markdownLayouts: [dirs]` builds the map: every `.mesa`/`.md` file directly in each directory, named by basename, with a later directory winning a name (ksite's mdsvex map had the same order). Layouts are a namespace apart from `autoImport`, as ksite's were, so `Trust.md` can say `layout: Trust`. The map reaches the Vite transform and the prerender alike. | <a id="fjs-1278"></a>FJS-1278 | sierra · junction · litestone — **A replayed write reaches the boundary with no statement of WHEN it was made, so a server that stamps `now()` dates a whole shift's work at the moment the network came back.** **Wants a ruling, not only a fix.** | 2026-09-29 | Built to FJS-D469. A held write now states when it was made: sierra's pending and attachment drains pass the entry's createdAt as madeAt, and the junction client sends it as X-Fjs-Made-At beside X-Fjs-Sent-At, which it reads off its own clock at the send. They go as headers over HTTP and in the frame's meta.headers over the socket. enterRequest is the one reader: resolveMadeAt moves the made-at by the device's offset (the server's now minus sentAt), so a clock that is wrong by a constant amount is corrected exactly. It refuses a made-at with no sent-at, one later than its sent-at, and an instant that does not parse, each with a 400. callService sets ctx.madeAt on every call, which is the arrival on a live call. installMadeAt hands litestone a closure over the request store through the new root db.$madeAt(fn), and @default(now()) and @updatedAt stamp from it. Predicates, @@expires and the audit trail stay on the landing time. Proved by junction test/made-at.test.ts (8 h back with a 3 h device skew, both transports, the refusals, the client headers), test/context-contract.test.ts § madeAt, litestone test/made-at.test.ts and sierra test/pending-queue.test.js. The sierra test goes red with the one line removed. All three suites are green and junction typechecks clean. fli prove: 15/28. Drop that line and strangers search for nothing, with no declaration to object** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled FJS-D514 A. methods: [{ method, gate: 0, claims: ['passId'] }] -- gateAuthAround grades every named claim present and non-null on what the resolver merged (guest 401, session 403, before the body); refused on a CRUD verb; svc.describe().methodClaims reports it. Pinned: junction test/method-claims.test.ts over HTTP. Portal's search still spells gate: 0 and should move to claims:. · [litestone.ts:2857](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) · [FJS-1412](#fjs-1412) · [FJS-1414](#fjs-1414) · [FJS-1413](#fjs-1413) · [portal api/test/passes.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/test/passes.test.ts) · [portal db/schema.lite § Privacy Pass](../fjs-prototypes/portal/db/schema.lite) · [portal core/pass-claim.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/core/pass-claim.ts) · [portal fakes/drive-pass.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/fakes/drive-pass.ts) · [IDEAS/bearer-access.md](IDEAS/bearer-access.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1462"></a>FJS-1462 | litestone · junction · basecamp — **A claims-only guest still grades above STRANGER wherever `getLevel` is not the scaffold's: Litestone's default `gradeStanding` answers CREATOR(3) for `{ ...claims }`, and basecamp's and the examples' hand-written `getLevel`s answer USER** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled FJS-D515 A. toolbelt isIdentified(): no id and no userId grades STRANGER in gradeStanding, and GatePlugin never asks an app getLevel about such a principal, so basecamp's and the example's hand-written getLevels are no longer reached for a guest. Pinned: toolbelt gate.spec 'no identity is a stranger', litestone elegance-fixes 'a resolver is never asked about a principal with no identity'. · [gate.js](packages/toolbelt/src/gate/gate.js) · [basecamp gate.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/core/gate.ts) · [litestone.ts `mergeClaims`](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-d520"></a>FJS-D520 | auth — **How does a row-tenanted app give a registering user their tenant — an onRegistered hook in the create transaction, createUser passing schema-declared User columns, or an app-side recipe?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D520`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d520): **A** — `onRegistered({ user, db })`, run inside the same transaction as the user insert; it may create the Account and membership and write the tenant column, and a throw rolls back the whole registration. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d518"></a>FJS-D518 | litestone — **Where does a data migration live: outside migrations/ (docs name seed/an app script), or a .js migration that declares rows-only and the shadow skips?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D518`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d518): **A** — A data change is not a migration. `migrations/` holds `.sql` only, and the docs name the home for a data change: an app script chained after `migrate apply` and made idempotent by the rows it expects, which is what remnant chose (`remnant/PLAN.md` Q2). `.js` in the history becomes a refusal with a nonzero exit. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d506"></a>FJS-D506 | litestone — **Should a numeric validator (@gte, @lte, @gt, @lt) also emit a column CHECK, so an atomic operator on that column is allowed rather than refused?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D506`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d506): **A** — Emit `CHECK (col >= n)` from each numeric validator, the way `@scale`/`@money` already emit theirs, and let the operator through. A migration, a seed, a raw statement and `asSystem()` are then held as well. The cost is a rebuild migration for every existing schema that carries one, and a CHECK violation needs the validator's message rather than SQLite's. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d521"></a>FJS-D521 | litestone — **Should a refused foreign key throw a new ForeignKeyError, or should one constraint error class carry model/field/value for unique, FK and datatype alike?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D521`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d521): **A** — `ForeignKeyError` beside `UniqueConflictError` in `core/errors.js`, built in `client.js` by checking the refused row's declared relations; datatype becomes a `ValidationError` before the write. junction gives it `status` 422. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d522"></a>FJS-D522 | junction — **How does an app compose two principal claim sources so each stays described in the principal snapshot?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D522`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d522): **A** — `principal` also takes an array: resolvers run in order, results merged, a claim name emitted by two refused, the snapshot describes each element (a plain function still reads "describes nothing", but only for itself). · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d515"></a>FJS-D515 | litestone — **Who grades a claims-only guest STRANGER — Litestone's gate for any principal without an id, a guest marker mergeClaims sets, or each app's getLevel?** | 2026-09-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D515`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d515): **A** — Litestone owns it: `GatePlugin` grades a principal with neither `id` nor `userId` STRANGER before any `getLevel` runs, and `gradeStanding` makes the same test so Junction's `sessionGateLevel` and Caravan agree. After `toDataPrincipal`, every session already carries `id`, so no real session changes grade. The cost: a test or app calling `$setAuth({ role: 'admin' })` without an `id` now grades 0, which is three call sites in `litestone.test.ts` and any more that grep finds. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |

## See also

- `ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md` — closed rows aged out of § Closed. Same table, verbatim. Read when tracing a specific id, never cold.
- `CLAUDE.md` § Live hazards — traps that are **correct behavior**; knowing them saves a day. Not issues.
- `DECISIONS.md` — settled rulings. A `FJS-D##` closing here lands there.
- `IDEAS/` — roadmap and design records for work not started. `IDEAS/overview.md`'s wave 0 is the subset of this file that happens to be argued in an ideas file.
- `HANDOFF.md` — per-session narrative, newest first. History, not a ledger.
- `VERIFYING.md` — how to promote a `stale?` row to `open` or `closed`.
